# Part 15 — Admin Panel + Reports + Blocking + Moderation + Account Deletion

Now we build the **safety and administration layer** for White Fiber.

This stage adds:

```text
Admin Dashboard
      │
      ├── Users
      ├── Reports
      ├── Posts
      ├── Comments
      ├── Blocks
      ├── Account deletion
      └── Moderation actions
```

Keep the project separation exactly as before:

```text
white-fiber/
├── frontend/
└── backend/
```

---

# 15.1 — Backend moderation app

Create:

```text
backend/moderation/
```

Structure:

```text
backend/
└── moderation/
    ├── __init__.py
    ├── admin.py
    ├── apps.py
    ├── models.py
    ├── serializers.py
    ├── urls.py
    ├── views.py
    └── migrations/
```

Create the app:

```bash
cd white-fiber/backend

python manage.py startapp moderation
```

Add it to:

```python
# backend/config/settings.py

INSTALLED_APPS = [
    # ...
    "moderation",
]
```

---

# 15.2 — User blocking

A user should be able to block another user.

Create in:

```text
backend/moderation/models.py
```

```python
from django.conf import settings
from django.db import models


class UserBlock(models.Model):
    blocker = models.ForeignKey(
        settings.AUTH_USER_MODEL,
        on_delete=models.CASCADE,
        related_name="blocks_created",
    )

    blocked = models.ForeignKey(
        settings.AUTH_USER_MODEL,
        on_delete=models.CASCADE,
        related_name="blocks_received",
    )

    created_at = models.DateTimeField(auto_now_add=True)

    class Meta:
        ordering = ["-created_at"]
        constraints = [
            models.UniqueConstraint(
                fields=["blocker", "blocked"],
                name="unique_user_block",
            ),
        ]

    def __str__(self):
        return (
            f"{self.blocker.username} blocked "
            f"{self.blocked.username}"
        )
```

---

# 15.3 — Prevent self-blocking

We don't want:

```text
John → blocks → John
```

Add validation in the serializer.

Create:

```text
backend/moderation/serializers.py
```

```python
from django.contrib.auth import get_user_model
from rest_framework import serializers

from .models import UserBlock

User = get_user_model()


class UserBlockSerializer(serializers.ModelSerializer):
    blocked_username = serializers.CharField(
        source="blocked.username",
        read_only=True,
    )

    class Meta:
        model = UserBlock
        fields = [
            "id",
            "blocked",
            "blocked_username",
            "created_at",
        ]
        read_only_fields = [
            "id",
            "created_at",
        ]

    def validate_blocked(self, value):
        request = self.context["request"]

        if value == request.user:
            raise serializers.ValidationError(
                "You cannot block yourself."
            )

        return value
```

---

# 15.4 — Block API

Create:

```text
backend/moderation/views.py
```

```python
from django.shortcuts import get_object_or_404
from rest_framework import status
from rest_framework.permissions import IsAuthenticated
from rest_framework.response import Response
from rest_framework.views import APIView

from users.models import User
from .models import UserBlock
from .serializers import UserBlockSerializer


class BlockUserView(APIView):
    permission_classes = [IsAuthenticated]

    def post(self, request, user_id):
        target = get_object_or_404(User, id=user_id)

        if target == request.user:
            return Response(
                {"detail": "You cannot block yourself."},
                status=status.HTTP_400_BAD_REQUEST,
            )

        block, created = UserBlock.objects.get_or_create(
            blocker=request.user,
            blocked=target,
        )

        serializer = UserBlockSerializer(
            block,
            context={"request": request},
        )

        return Response(
            serializer.data,
            status=(
                status.HTTP_201_CREATED
                if created
                else status.HTTP_200_OK
            ),
        )


class UnblockUserView(APIView):
    permission_classes = [IsAuthenticated]

    def delete(self, request, user_id):
        deleted, _ = UserBlock.objects.filter(
            blocker=request.user,
            blocked_id=user_id,
        ).delete()

        if not deleted:
            return Response(
                {"detail": "User is not blocked."},
                status=status.HTTP_404_NOT_FOUND,
            )

        return Response(status=status.HTTP_204_NO_CONTENT)


class BlockedUsersView(APIView):
    permission_classes = [IsAuthenticated]

    def get(self, request):
        blocks = UserBlock.objects.filter(
            blocker=request.user
        ).select_related("blocked")

        serializer = UserBlockSerializer(
            blocks,
            many=True,
            context={"request": request},
        )

        return Response(serializer.data)
```

---

# 15.5 — Block URLs

Create:

```text
backend/moderation/urls.py
```

```python
from django.urls import path

from .views import (
    BlockUserView,
    UnblockUserView,
    BlockedUsersView,
)

urlpatterns = [
    path(
        "users/<int:user_id>/block/",
        BlockUserView.as_view(),
        name="block-user",
    ),
    path(
        "users/<int:user_id>/block/",
        UnblockUserView.as_view(),
        name="unblock-user",
    ),
    path(
        "blocks/",
        BlockedUsersView.as_view(),
        name="blocked-users",
    ),
]
```

There is an issue here: Django cannot distinguish two identical URL patterns by HTTP method alone in this configuration.

Use one view instead.

Replace with:

```python
from django.urls import path

from .views import (
    BlockUserView,
    BlockedUsersView,
)

urlpatterns = [
    path(
        "users/<int:user_id>/block/",
        BlockUserView.as_view(),
        name="block-user",
    ),
    path(
        "blocks/",
        BlockedUsersView.as_view(),
        name="blocked-users",
    ),
]
```

And `BlockUserView` handles both:

```python
class BlockUserView(APIView):
    permission_classes = [IsAuthenticated]

    def post(self, request, user_id):
        target = get_object_or_404(User, id=user_id)

        if target == request.user:
            return Response(
                {"detail": "You cannot block yourself."},
                status=status.HTTP_400_BAD_REQUEST,
            )

        block, created = UserBlock.objects.get_or_create(
            blocker=request.user,
            blocked=target,
        )

        serializer = UserBlockSerializer(
            block,
            context={"request": request},
        )

        return Response(
            serializer.data,
            status=(
                status.HTTP_201_CREATED
                if created
                else status.HTTP_200_OK
            ),
        )

    def delete(self, request, user_id):
        deleted, _ = UserBlock.objects.filter(
            blocker=request.user,
            blocked_id=user_id,
        ).delete()

        if not deleted:
            return Response(
                {"detail": "User is not blocked."},
                status=status.HTTP_404_NOT_FOUND,
            )

        return Response(status=status.HTTP_204_NO_CONTENT)
```

# 15.6 — Register moderation URLs

In:

```text
backend/config/urls.py
```

add:

```python
from django.urls import include, path

urlpatterns = [
    # ...
    path(
        "api/moderation/",
        include("moderation.urls"),
    ),
]
```

Your API becomes:

```text
POST   /api/moderation/users/<id>/block/
DELETE /api/moderation/users/<id>/block/
GET    /api/moderation/blocks/
```

# 15.7 — Report system

Now create a general reporting system.

A report can target:

```text
User
Post
Comment
```

Model:

```python
class Report(models.Model):
    class Status(models.TextChoices):
        PENDING = "pending", "Pending"
        REVIEWING = "reviewing", "Reviewing"
        RESOLVED = "resolved", "Resolved"
        DISMISSED = "dismissed", "Dismissed"

    class Reason(models.TextChoices):
        SPAM = "spam", "Spam"
        HARASSMENT = "harassment", "Harassment"
        ABUSE = "abuse", "Abuse"
        HATE = "hate", "Hate"
        SEXUAL = "sexual", "Sexual Content"
        VIOLENCE = "violence", "Violence"
        IMPERSONATION = "impersonation", "Impersonation"
        SCAM = "scam", "Scam"
        OTHER = "other", "Other"

    reporter = models.ForeignKey(
        settings.AUTH_USER_MODEL,
        on_delete=models.CASCADE,
        related_name="submitted_reports",
    )

    reported_user = models.ForeignKey(
        settings.AUTH_USER_MODEL,
        on_delete=models.CASCADE,
        related_name="user_reports",
        null=True,
        blank=True,
    )

    post = models.ForeignKey(
        "posts.Post",
        on_delete=models.CASCADE,
        null=True,
        blank=True,
        related_name="reports",
    )

    comment = models.ForeignKey(
        "posts.Comment",
        on_delete=models.CASCADE,
        null=True,
        blank=True,
        related_name="reports",
    )

    reason = models.CharField(
        max_length=30,
        choices=Reason.choices,
    )

    description = models.TextField(
        blank=True,
        max_length=2000,
    )

    status = models.CharField(
        max_length=20,
        choices=Status.choices,
        default=Status.PENDING,
    )

    moderator_notes = models.TextField(
        blank=True,
    )

    created_at = models.DateTimeField(
        auto_now_add=True
    )

    updated_at = models.DateTimeField(
        auto_now=True
    )

    resolved_at = models.DateTimeField(
        null=True,
        blank=True,
    )

    class Meta:
        ordering = ["-created_at"]
        indexes = [
            models.Index(
                fields=["status", "-created_at"]
            ),
        ]
```

---

# 15.8 — Validate report targets

A report should contain at least one target.

Valid:

```text
reported_user
OR
post
OR
comment
```

Invalid:

```text
nothing
```

Also don't allow multiple unrelated targets in one report unless you deliberately design for that.

Serializer:

```python
class ReportSerializer(serializers.ModelSerializer):
    class Meta:
        model = Report
        fields = [
            "id",
            "reported_user",
            "post",
            "comment",
            "reason",
            "description",
            "status",
            "created_at",
        ]
        read_only_fields = [
            "id",
            "status",
            "created_at",
        ]

    def validate(self, attrs):
        targets = [
            attrs.get("reported_user"),
            attrs.get("post"),
            attrs.get("comment"),
        ]

        if sum(target is not None for target in targets) != 1:
            raise serializers.ValidationError(
                "Select exactly one report target."
            )

        return attrs

    def create(self, validated_data):
        return Report.objects.create(
            reporter=self.context["request"].user,
            **validated_data,
        )
```

---

# 15.9 — Report API

Add:

```python
from rest_framework.generics import CreateAPIView

class CreateReportView(CreateAPIView):
    permission_classes = [IsAuthenticated]
    serializer_class = ReportSerializer

    def get_serializer_context(self):
        return {
            **super().get_serializer_context(),
            "request": self.request,
        }
```

URL:

```python
path(
    "reports/",
    CreateReportView.as_view(),
    name="create-report",
),
```

The user can now submit:

```text
POST /api/moderation/reports/
```

Example:

```json
{
  "post": 123,
  "reason": "spam",
  "description": "This post appears to be spam."
}
```

# 15.10 — Admin interface

Now make moderation manageable through Django Admin.

`backend/moderation/admin.py`

```python
from django.contrib import admin

from .models import Report, UserBlock


@admin.register(UserBlock)
class UserBlockAdmin(admin.ModelAdmin):
    list_display = (
        "blocker",
        "blocked",
        "created_at",
    )

    search_fields = (
        "blocker__username",
        "blocked__username",
    )

    list_filter = (
        "created_at",
    )


@admin.register(Report)
class ReportAdmin(admin.ModelAdmin):
    list_display = (
        "id",
        "reporter",
        "reason",
        "status",
        "created_at",
    )

    list_filter = (
        "status",
        "reason",
        "created_at",
    )

    search_fields = (
        "reporter__username",
        "reported_user__username",
        "description",
    )

    readonly_fields = (
        "reporter",
        "created_at",
        "updated_at",
    )
```

Now administrators can review reports.

# 15.12 — Add admin actions

Useful actions:

```text
Resolve report
Dismiss report
Mark reviewing
```

Example:

```python
@admin.action(description="Mark selected reports as reviewing")
def mark_reviewing(modeladmin, request, queryset):
    queryset.update(
        status=Report.Status.REVIEWING
    )


@admin.action(description="Dismiss selected reports")
def dismiss_reports(modeladmin, request, queryset):
    queryset.update(
        status=Report.Status.DISMISSED
    )
```

Then:

```python
@admin.register(Report)
class ReportAdmin(admin.ModelAdmin):
    # ...

    actions = [
        mark_reviewing,
        dismiss_reports,
    ]
```

# 15.14 — Account deletion

Now add account deletion.

Recommended API:

```text
DELETE /api/users/me/delete/
```

In your users application:

```python
from rest_framework.permissions import IsAuthenticated
from rest_framework.response import Response
from rest_framework.views import APIView


class DeleteAccountView(APIView):
    permission_classes = [IsAuthenticated]

    def delete(self, request):
        user = request.user

        user.delete()

        return Response(
            {"detail": "Account deleted successfully."},
            status=204,
        )
```

URL:

```python
path(
    "me/delete/",
    DeleteAccountView.as_view(),
    name="delete-account",
),
```

---

# 15.15 — Think carefully about deletion

When:

```python
user.delete()
```

runs, related records depend on their `on_delete` configuration.

You should review:

```text
Posts
Comments
Reactions
Friends
Friend requests
Conversations
Messages
Notifications
Device tokens
Uploaded files
Reports
Blocks
```

For example, some data should potentially be deleted with the account while other records may need anonymization or retention depending on your policy and legal requirements.

Do not assume Django's cascading behavior automatically gives you the exact privacy behavior you want.

---

# 15.16 — Delete device tokens

Before/while deleting an account, make sure registered push devices disappear.

For example:

```python
from notifications.models import UserDevice

UserDevice.objects.filter(
    user=request.user
).delete()
```

This prevents an old account's push token from remaining registered.

```javascript
import api from "./api";

export async function deleteAccount() {
  const response = await api.delete("/users/me/delete/");
  return response.data;
}
```

Then after successful deletion:

```javascript
localStorage.removeItem("access_token");
localStorage.removeItem("refresh_token");
```

And redirect to:

```text
/login
```

# 15.19 — Block users from the frontend

Add to a profile menu:

```text
⋮
├── View Profile
├── Message
├── Report
└── Block
```

Block:

```javascript
await api.post(
  `/moderation/users/${userId}/block/`
);
```

Unblock:

```javascript
await api.delete(
  `/moderation/users/${userId}/block/`
);
```

---

# 15.20 — Hide blocked users from feeds

This is important.

If:

```text
John blocks Sarah
```

Sarah's content should generally not appear to John.

You need to apply block filtering to relevant querysets.

For example:

```python
blocked_ids = UserBlock.objects.filter(
    blocker=request.user
).values_list(
    "blocked_id",
    flat=True,
)
```

Then:

```python
posts = Post.objects.exclude(
    author_id__in=blocked_ids
)
```

Also consider the reverse direction:

```python
blocked_by_ids = UserBlock.objects.filter(
    blocked=request.user
).values_list(
    "blocker_id",
    flat=True,
)
```

Then exclude both directions where appropriate:

```python
excluded_user_ids = set(blocked_ids) | set(blocked_by_ids)
```

This prevents blocked relationships from leaking through:

* Feed
* Search
* Friend suggestions
* Comments
* Profiles
* Messaging

---

# 15.21 — Block messaging

When a user blocks another user, messaging should also respect the block.

Before creating a conversation:

```python
if UserBlock.objects.filter(
    blocker=user_one,
    blocked=user_two,
).exists():
    raise PermissionError(
        "Messaging is unavailable."
    )

if UserBlock.objects.filter(
    blocker=user_two,
    blocked=user_one,
).exists():
    raise PermissionError(
        "Messaging is unavailable."
    )
```

This prevents:

```text
Block user
     ↓
still create new conversation
     ↓
❌
```

---

# 15.22 — WebSocket block protection

This is especially important.

Your WebSocket consumer must not only verify JWT authentication.

It should also verify that the participants are allowed to communicate.

Conceptually:

```python
if UserBlock.objects.filter(
    blocker=request_user,
    blocked=other_user,
).exists():
    await self.close(code=4003)
```

And check the reverse direction as well.

Never rely solely on frontend blocking.

# 15.24 — Moderation workflow

Your final moderation workflow becomes:

```text
User
 │
 │ Report
 ▼
Django
 │
 ▼
Report
 │
 ▼
PENDING
 │
 ▼
Admin reviews
 │
 ├── Dismiss
 │
 ├── Resolve
 │
 └── Take moderation action
```

Admin can then:

```text
Report
 ├── inspect user
 ├── inspect post
 ├── inspect comment
 ├── review history
 ├── suspend account
 └── remove content
```

---

# 15.25 — Add user suspension

For serious moderation, your custom User model should have:

```python
is_suspended = models.BooleanField(
    default=False
)

suspended_until = models.DateTimeField(
    null=True,
    blank=True
)
```

Then your authentication layer can reject suspended users.

For permanent suspension:

```text
is_suspended = True
suspended_until = NULL
```

For temporary suspension:

```text
is_suspended = True
suspended_until = 2026-10-01T00:00:00
```

---

# 15.26 — Don't only hide suspended users in React

This must be enforced server-side.

Bad:

```text
React:
"If suspended, don't show the button."
```

Good:

```text
React
  +
Django permission/authentication layer
```

The backend must reject actions from suspended accounts.

---

But for now, Django Admin can handle the first production moderation workflow.

At the end of Part 15, White Fiber has the essential **trust, safety, moderation, blocking, and account-management foundation** expected of a public social network.

**Next stage: Part 16 — Advanced Admin Dashboard + Analytics + User Management + Moderation UI + Platform Statistics.**


				                             part 5

# Part 16 — Advanced Admin Dashboard + Analytics + User Management

Now we build the **White Fiber administration system** on top of Part 15.

The goal is to move from basic Django Admin to a dedicated management dashboard:

```text
White Fiber Admin
│
├── Dashboard
│   ├── Total users
│   ├── Active users
│   ├── New users
│   ├── Posts
│   ├── Messages
│   ├── Reports
│   └── Growth
│
├── Users
│   ├── Search
│   ├── View
│   ├── Suspend
│   ├── Unsuspend
│   └── Delete
│
├── Reports
│   ├── Pending
│   ├── Reviewing
│   ├── Resolved
│   └── Dismissed
│
├── Content
│   ├── Posts
│   └── Comments
│
└── System
    ├── Notifications
    ├── Devices
    └── Health
```

We will keep the same strict separation:

```text
white-fiber/
├── frontend/
└── backend/
```

---

# 16.1 — Admin architecture

For the first production version, use:

```text
React Admin UI
       │
       ▼
Django Admin API
       │
       ├── Users
       ├── Reports
       ├── Posts
       ├── Comments
       └── Analytics
```

Do **not** put admin logic directly into the public user endpoints.

Create a dedicated backend area:

```text
backend/
└── administration/
```

---

```text
    ├── admin.py
    ├── apps.py

    ├── serializers.py

```

Add it:

```python
# backend/config/settings.py

INSTALLED_APPS = [
    # ...
    "administration",
]
```

# 16.3 — Admin permission

Only staff should access these endpoints.

Create:

```text
backend/administration/permissions.py
```

```python
from rest_framework.permissions import BasePermission


class IsAdminUser(BasePermission):
    message = "Administrator access is required."

    def has_permission(self, request, view):
        return bool(
            request.user
            and request.user.is_authenticated
            and request.user.is_staff
        )
```

This means:

```text
normal user → ❌
staff user  → ✅
superuser   → ✅
```

---

# 16.4 — Dashboard statistics

Create:

```text
backend/administration/views.py
```

```python
from django.contrib.auth import get_user_model
from django.db.models import Count
from django.utils import timezone
from rest_framework.permissions import IsAuthenticated
from rest_framework.response import Response
from rest_framework.views import APIView

from moderation.models import Report
from posts.models import Post
from messaging.models import Message

from .permissions import IsAdminUser

User = get_user_model()


class AdminDashboardView(APIView):
    permission_classes = [
        IsAuthenticated,
        IsAdminUser,
    ]

    def get(self, request):
        now = timezone.now()

        today = now.date()

        total_users = User.objects.count()

        active_users = User.objects.filter(
            is_active=True
        ).count()

        new_users_today = User.objects.filter(
            date_joined__date=today
        ).count()

        total_posts = Post.objects.count()

        total_messages = Message.objects.count()

        pending_reports = Report.objects.filter(
            status=Report.Status.PENDING
        ).count()

        return Response({
            "users": {
                "total": total_users,
                "active": active_users,
                "new_today": new_users_today,
            },
            "content": {
                "posts": total_posts,
            },
            "messages": {
                "total": total_messages,
            },
            "reports": {
                "pending": pending_reports,
            },
        })
```

```python
urlpatterns = [
```

```python
),
```

Your endpoint is now:

```text

```

# 16.7 — Admin user management

Create:

```text
GET /api/admin/users/
```

Capabilities:

```text
Search
Pagination
Status filter
Staff filter
Suspended filter
```

View:

```python
from rest_framework.generics import ListAPIView

from .permissions import IsAdminUser
from users.models import User
from users.serializers import UserSerializer


class AdminUserListView(ListAPIView):
    permission_classes = [
        IsAuthenticated,
        IsAdminUser,
    ]

    serializer_class = UserSerializer

    def get_queryset(self):
        queryset = User.objects.all().order_by(
            "-date_joined"
        )

        search = self.request.query_params.get(
            "q"
        )

        if search:
            queryset = queryset.filter(
                username__icontains=search
            )

        status_filter = self.request.query_params.get(
            "status"
        )

        if status_filter == "active":
            queryset = queryset.filter(
                is_active=True
            )

        elif status_filter == "inactive":
            queryset = queryset.filter(
                is_active=False
            )

        return queryset
```

---

# 16.8 — Admin user details

Add:

```text
GET /api/admin/users/<id>/
```

Return useful administrative information:

```json
{
  "id": 25,
  "username": "john",
  "email": "john@example.com",
  "is_active": true,
  "is_staff": false,
  "is_suspended": false,
  "date_joined": "...",
  "last_login": "...",
  "post_count": 42,
  "friend_count": 18
}
```

Avoid returning sensitive information unnecessarily.

Never expose:

```text
password
password hash
JWT tokens
refresh tokens
private credentials
```

---

# 16.9 — Suspend user API

Add:

```text
POST /api/admin/users/<id>/suspend/
```

Example:

```python
from rest_framework import status


class AdminSuspendUserView(APIView):
    permission_classes = [
        IsAuthenticated,
        IsAdminUser,
    ]

    def post(self, request, user_id):
        user = get_object_or_404(
            User,
            id=user_id,
        )

        if user == request.user:
            return Response(
                {
                    "detail":
                    "You cannot suspend yourself."
                },
                status=status.HTTP_400_BAD_REQUEST,
            )

        user.is_suspended = True
        user.suspended_until = None
        user.save(
            update_fields=[
                "is_suspended",
                "suspended_until",
            ]
        )

        return Response({
            "detail": "User suspended."
        })
```

---

# 16.10 — Unsuspend user

Add:

```text
POST /api/admin/users/<id>/unsuspend/
```

```python
class AdminUnsuspendUserView(APIView):
    permission_classes = [
        IsAuthenticated,
        IsAdminUser,
    ]

    def post(self, request, user_id):
        user = get_object_or_404(
            User,
            id=user_id,
        )

        user.is_suspended = False
        user.suspended_until = None

        user.save(
            update_fields=[
                "is_suspended",
                "suspended_until",
            ]
        )

        return Response({
            "detail": "User unsuspended."
        })
```

# 16.11 — Admin report management

Now expose the reports from Part 15.

Endpoints:

```text
GET   /api/admin/reports/
GET   /api/admin/reports/<id>/
PATCH /api/admin/reports/<id>/
```

Example queryset:

```python
class AdminReportListView(ListAPIView):
    permission_classes = [
        IsAuthenticated,
        IsAdminUser,
    ]

    serializer_class = ReportSerializer

    def get_queryset(self):
        queryset = (
            Report.objects
            .select_related(
                "reporter",
                "reported_user",
                "post",
                "comment",
            )
            .order_by("-created_at")
        )

        status_filter = (
            self.request.query_params.get("status")
        )

        if status_filter:
            queryset = queryset.filter(
                status=status_filter
            )

        return queryset
```

# 16.12 — Moderator actions

Your dashboard should allow:

```text
Pending
   ↓
Reviewing
   ↓
Resolved
```

or:

```text
Pending
   ↓
Dismissed
```

For example:

```json
{
  "status": "resolved",
  "moderator_notes": "Reviewed and action taken."
}
```

Only administrators should be able to change moderation status.

---

# 16.13 — Content moderation

Add administrative actions for posts.

For example:

```text
GET    /api/admin/posts/
DELETE /api/admin/posts/<id>/
```

Before deleting content, you may want to record:

```text
Who deleted it
When
Why
Which report caused the action
```

This is better than silently deleting records.

---

# 16.14 — Moderation audit log

Create:

```text
backend/administration/models.py
```

```python
from django.conf import settings
from django.db import models


class AdminActionLog(models.Model):
    admin = models.ForeignKey(
        settings.AUTH_USER_MODEL,
        on_delete=models.SET_NULL,
        null=True,
        related_name="admin_actions",
    )

    action = models.CharField(
        max_length=100
    )

    target_type = models.CharField(
        max_length=100
    )

    target_id = models.PositiveIntegerField(
        null=True,
        blank=True,
    )

    reason = models.TextField(
        blank=True
    )

    created_at = models.DateTimeField(
        auto_now_add=True
    )

    class Meta:
        ordering = ["-created_at"]
```

Now you can record:

```text
Admin
   │
   ├── suspended user 52
   ├── deleted post 841
   ├── resolved report 21
   └── dismissed report 22
```

---

# 16.15 — Why audit logs matter

Suppose a moderator deletes a post.

Instead of:

```text
Post disappears
```

you have:

```text
Admin Action
────────────────────────
Admin: moderator01
Action: delete_post
Target: Post #841
Reason: Policy violation
Time: 2026-09-24 18:20
```

This gives you an administrative history.

# 16.19 — Protect the admin routes

Create:

```text
frontend/src/routes/AdminRoute.jsx
```

```jsx
import { Navigate, Outlet } from "react-router-dom";
import { useAuth } from "../hooks/useAuth";

export default function AdminRoute() {
  const { user, loading } = useAuth();

  if (loading) {
    return <div>Loading...</div>;
  }

  if (!user) {
    return <Navigate to="/login" replace />;
  }

  if (!user.is_staff) {
    return <Navigate to="/" replace />;
  }

  return <Outlet />;
}
```

Important:

This is **not** your real security boundary.

The backend still checks:

```python
request.user.is_staff
```

The frontend check is only for navigation/UI.

# 16.21 — Admin service

Create:

```text
frontend/src/services/adminService.js
```

```javascript
import api from "./api";

export async function getDashboard() {
  const response = await api.get(
    "/admin/dashboard/"
  );

  return response.data;
}

export async function getAdminUsers(params = {}) {
  const response = await api.get(
    "/admin/users/",
    { params }
  );

  return response.data;
}

export async function suspendUser(userId) {
  const response = await api.post(
    `/admin/users/${userId}/suspend/`
  );

  return response.data;
}

export async function unsuspendUser(userId) {
  const response = await api.post(
    `/admin/users/${userId}/unsuspend/`
  );

  return response.data;
}

export async function getAdminReports(params = {}) {
  const response = await api.get(
    "/admin/reports/",
    { params }
  );

  return response.data;
}
```

# 16.23 — Admin dashboard page

```jsx
import { useEffect, useState } from "react";
import StatCard from "../../components/admin/StatCard";
import {
  getDashboard,
} from "../../services/adminService";

export default function AdminDashboard() {
  const [data, setData] = useState(null);
  const [loading, setLoading] = useState(true);

  useEffect(() => {
    getDashboard()
      .then(setData)
      .finally(() => setLoading(false));
  }, []);

  if (loading) {
    return <div>Loading dashboard...</div>;
  }

  if (!data) {
    return <div>Unable to load dashboard.</div>;
  }

  return (
    <section>
      <h1>Dashboard</h1>

      <div className="stats-grid">
        <StatCard
          title="Total Users"
          value={data.users.total}
        />

        <StatCard
          title="New Today"
          value={data.users.new_today}
        />

        <StatCard
          title="Posts"
          value={data.content.posts}
        />

        <StatCard
          title="Messages"
          value={data.messages.total}
        />

        <StatCard
          title="Pending Reports"
          value={data.reports.pending}
        />
      </div>
    </section>
  );
}
```

# 16.29 — User growth API

Add:

```text
GET /api/admin/analytics/users/
```

Example implementation:

```python
from datetime import timedelta

from django.db.models.functions import TruncDate
from django.db.models import Count
from django.utils import timezone


class UserAnalyticsView(APIView):
    permission_classes = [
        IsAuthenticated,
        IsAdminUser,
    ]

    def get(self, request):
        end = timezone.now()
        start = end - timedelta(days=30)

        data = (
            User.objects
            .filter(date_joined__gte=start)
            .annotate(day=TruncDate("date_joined"))
            .values("day")
            .annotate(count=Count("id"))
            .order_by("day")
        )

        return Response(list(data))
```

---

# 16.30 — Admin analytics frontend

You can use a chart library later, but initially keep the API simple.

Response:

```json
[
  {
    "day": "2026-09-20",
    "count": 24
  },
  {
    "day": "2026-09-21",
    "count": 31
  },
  {
    "day": "2026-09-22",
    "count": 19
  }
]
```

This allows the frontend to display:

```text
Users
  ^
35|       ●
30|    ●
25| ●
20|            ●
15|
  +------------------>
    20  21  22  23
```

---

# 16.31 — Don't count everything on every request forever

For a small application:

```text
database aggregation → acceptable
```

At large scale:

```text
millions of users
millions of posts
millions of messages
```

you'll eventually want:

```text
Analytics events
       │
       ▼
Queue
       │
       ▼
Analytics storage
       │
       ▼
Dashboard
```

For White Fiber's initial production release, database aggregation is simpler.

---

# 16.32 — Admin notifications

Add a dashboard indicator:

```text
Reports
   🔴 14
```

When a new report arrives:

```text
Pending Reports: 15
```

Later you can add WebSocket-based admin notifications.

For now, polling every 30–60 seconds is sufficient.

---

# 16.33 — Admin security

The admin dashboard contains sensitive information.

Implement:

```text
[ ] HTTPS only
[ ] JWT authentication
[ ] is_staff backend permission
[ ] Strong admin passwords
[ ] 2FA where supported
[ ] Audit logs
[ ] Rate limiting
[ ] No password exposure
[ ] No token exposure
[ ] No unrestricted database endpoints
```

Most importantly:

```text
Frontend:
"is_staff"

        +

Backend:
request.user.is_staff
```

The backend is authoritative.

---

# 16.34 — Add admin API rate limiting

Administrative endpoints should have their own throttle.

Example:

```python
from rest_framework.throttling import UserRateThrottle


class AdminRateThrottle(UserRateThrottle):
    scope = "admin"
```

Settings:

```python
REST_FRAMEWORK = {
    # ...

    "DEFAULT_THROTTLE_RATES": {
        "anon": "30/minute",
        "user": "120/minute",
        "admin": "300/minute",
    },
}
```

Then:

```python
throttle_classes = [
    AdminRateThrottle
]
```

Use appropriate limits for your actual traffic.

---

# 16.35 — Audit every dangerous action

Log:

```text
Suspend user
Unsuspend user
Delete post
Delete comment
Resolve report
Dismiss report
Change moderation status
```

Example:

```python
AdminActionLog.objects.create(
    admin=request.user,
    action="suspend_user",
    target_type="User",
    target_id=user.id,
    reason=request.data.get(
        "reason",
        ""
    ),
)
```

This gives you accountability.

---

# 16.36 — Add reason to suspension

Instead of:

```json
{
  "action": "suspend"
}
```

use:

```json
{
  "reason": "Repeated spam reports."
}
```

Then save it in the audit log.

The user-facing suspension response can remain generic while the internal record contains the administrative reason.

```text
Suspension

POST /api/admin/users/<id>/unsuspend/

Reports
GET   /api/admin/reports/
GET   /api/admin/reports/<id>/
PATCH /api/admin/reports/<id>/

Analytics
GET /api/admin/analytics/users/

Content
GET    /api/admin/posts/
DELETE /api/admin/posts/<id>/
```

# 17.2 — Search Services

Create:

`backend/discovery/services.py`

```python
from django.db.models import Q
from django.contrib.auth import get_user_model

from posts.models import Post
from moderation.models import UserBlock

User = get_user_model()


def blocked_user_ids(user):
    blocked_by_me = UserBlock.objects.filter(
        blocker=user
    ).values_list("blocked_id", flat=True)

    blocked_me = UserBlock.objects.filter(
        blocked=user
    ).values_list("blocker_id", flat=True)

    return set(blocked_by_me).union(set(blocked_me))


def search_users(user, query):
    blocked_ids = blocked_user_ids(user)

    return (
        User.objects
        .filter(
            Q(username__icontains=query)
            | Q(first_name__icontains=query)
            | Q(last_name__icontains=query)
        )
        .exclude(id=user.id)
        .exclude(id__in=blocked_ids)
        .filter(is_active=True)
        .order_by("username")
    )


def search_posts(user, query):
    blocked_ids = blocked_user_ids(user)

    return (
        Post.objects
        .filter(
            Q(content__icontains=query)
            | Q(author__username__icontains=query)
        )
        .exclude(author_id__in=blocked_ids)
        .filter(author__is_active=True)
        .order_by("-created_at")
        .distinct()
    )
```

This makes blocking **server-side**, rather than relying on React to hide results.

---

# 17.3 — User Search Serializer

Create:

`backend/discovery/serializers.py`

```python
from rest_framework import serializers

from django.contrib.auth import get_user_model

from posts.models import Post

User = get_user_model()


class SearchUserSerializer(serializers.ModelSerializer):
    class Meta:
        model = User
        fields = [
            "id",
            "username",
            "first_name",
            "last_name",
            "avatar",
            "is_online",
            "last_seen",
        ]


class SearchPostSerializer(serializers.ModelSerializer):
    author = SearchUserSerializer(read_only=True)

    class Meta:
        model = Post
        fields = [
            "id",
            "author",
            "content",
            "created_at",
            "updated_at",
        ]
```

> If your custom `User` model uses a different avatar field, replace `"avatar"` with your actual field name.

# 17.4 — Search API

Create:

`backend/discovery/views.py`

```python
from rest_framework.views import APIView
from rest_framework.permissions import IsAuthenticated
from rest_framework.response import Response

from .services import search_users, search_posts
from .serializers import (
    SearchUserSerializer,
    SearchPostSerializer,
)


class UserSearchView(APIView):
    permission_classes = [IsAuthenticated]

    def get(self, request):
        query = request.query_params.get("q", "").strip()

        if len(query) < 2:
            return Response({
                "results": [],
                "message": "Enter at least 2 characters.",
            })

        users = search_users(request.user, query)

        return Response({
            "results": SearchUserSerializer(
                users[:20],
                many=True,
            ).data,
        })


class PostSearchView(APIView):
    permission_classes = [IsAuthenticated]

    def get(self, request):
        query = request.query_params.get("q", "").strip()

        if len(query) < 2:
            return Response({
                "results": [],
                "message": "Enter at least 2 characters.",
            })

        posts = search_posts(request.user, query)

        return Response({
            "results": SearchPostSerializer(
                posts[:20],
                many=True,
            ).data,
        })
```

# 17.6 — User Suggestions

Now implement Facebook-style friend suggestions.

The initial algorithm will consider:

1. Mutual friends
2. Exclude yourself
3. Exclude existing friends
4. Exclude pending requests
5. Exclude blocked users
6. Exclude inactive/suspended accounts

Add to:

`backend/discovery/services.py`

```python
from friends.models import Friendship, FriendRequest


def get_user_suggestions(user, limit=20):
    blocked_ids = blocked_user_ids(user)

    friend_ids = set(
        Friendship.objects.filter(
            Q(user1=user) | Q(user2=user)
        ).values_list("user1_id", "user2_id")
    )

    normalized_friend_ids = set()

    for pair in friend_ids:
        normalized_friend_ids.update(pair)

    normalized_friend_ids.discard(user.id)

    pending_ids = set(
        FriendRequest.objects.filter(
            Q(sender=user) | Q(receiver=user),
            status=FriendRequest.Status.PENDING,
        ).values_list("sender_id", "receiver_id")
    )

    normalized_pending_ids = set()

    for pair in pending_ids:
        normalized_pending_ids.update(pair)

    normalized_pending_ids.discard(user.id)

    excluded_ids = (
        blocked_ids
        | normalized_friend_ids
        | normalized_pending_ids
        | {user.id}
    )

    candidates = (
        User.objects
        .filter(is_active=True)
        .exclude(id__in=excluded_ids)
        .order_by("?")[:limit]
    )

    return candidates
```

### Important

If your `Friendship` or `FriendRequest` model uses different field names, use those existing names instead.

For production at large scale, avoid `order_by("?")`; later we can replace it with a scored recommendation system.

# 17.8 — Hashtag Support

If Part 7 already created your hashtag model, **reuse that model** rather than creating a second hashtag table.

A clean model is:

`backend/posts/models.py`

```python
class Hashtag(models.Model):
    name = models.CharField(
        max_length=100,
        unique=True,
        db_index=True,
    )

    created_at = models.DateTimeField(
        auto_now_add=True,
    )

    def __str__(self):
        return f"#{self.name}"
```

Then connect it to posts:

```python
class Post(models.Model):
    # existing fields...

    hashtags = models.ManyToManyField(
        Hashtag,
        blank=True,
        related_name="posts",
    )
```

Run:

```bash
python manage.py makemigrations
python manage.py migrate
```

---

# 17.9 — Extract Hashtags Automatically

Add:

`backend/posts/hashtag_utils.py`

```python
import re

HASHTAG_PATTERN = re.compile(
    r"#([A-Za-z0-9_]+)"
)


def extract_hashtags(content):
    return {
        match.lower()
        for match in HASHTAG_PATTERN.findall(content)
    }
```

Example:

```text
"Having fun in #Lahore #WhiteFiber!"
```

becomes:

```python
{
    "lahore",
    "whitefiber",
}
```

---

# 17.10 — Attach Hashtags to Posts

Where your post is created, after saving the post:

```python
from .hashtag_utils import extract_hashtags
from .models import Hashtag
```

Then:

```python
tags = extract_hashtags(post.content)

for tag in tags:
    hashtag, _ = Hashtag.objects.get_or_create(
        name=tag
    )

    post.hashtags.add(hashtag)
```

This keeps hashtag creation automatic.

# 17.11 — Hashtag Search

Add to:

`backend/discovery/views.py`

```python
from posts.models import Hashtag, Post


class HashtagView(APIView):
    permission_classes = [IsAuthenticated]

    def get(self, request, tag):
        tag = tag.strip().lower()

        try:
            hashtag = Hashtag.objects.get(
                name=tag
            )
        except Hashtag.DoesNotExist:
            return Response({
                "hashtag": tag,
                "posts": [],
            })

        blocked_ids = blocked_user_ids(request.user)

        posts = (
            Post.objects
            .filter(
                hashtags=hashtag,
            )
            .exclude(
                author_id__in=blocked_ids
            )
            .filter(
                author__is_active=True
            )
            .order_by("-created_at")
            .distinct()
        )

        return Response({
            "hashtag": tag,
            "posts": SearchPostSerializer(
                posts[:50],
                many=True,
            ).data,
        })
```

URL:

```python
path(
    "hashtags/<str:tag>/",
    HashtagView.as_view(),
    name="hashtag-detail",
),
```

API:

```text
GET /api/hashtags/python/
GET /api/hashtags/whitefiber/
GET /api/hashtags/lahore/
```

# 17.12 — Trending Hashtags

Create:

```python
from django.db.models import Count
from django.utils import timezone
from datetime import timedelta
```

Add:

```python
class TrendingHashtagsView(APIView):
    permission_classes = [IsAuthenticated]

    def get(self, request):
        since = timezone.now() - timedelta(hours=24)

        hashtags = (
            Hashtag.objects
            .filter(
                posts__created_at__gte=since,
                posts__author__is_active=True,
            )
            .annotate(
                post_count=Count(
                    "posts",
                    distinct=True,
                )
            )
            .order_by(
                "-post_count",
                "name",
            )[:20]
        )

        return Response({
            "results": [
                {
                    "name": hashtag.name,
                    "post_count": hashtag.post_count,
                }
                for hashtag in hashtags
            ],
        })
```

URL:

```python
path(
    "hashtags/trending/",
    TrendingHashtagsView.as_view(),
    name="trending-hashtags",
),
```

Endpoint:

```text
GET /api/hashtags/trending/
```

This is deliberately a simple, transparent first version:

```text
Trending score ≈ number of recent posts
```

Later we can make it more sophisticated using reactions, comments, velocity, and recency.

# 17.13 — Explore API

Create:

```python
class ExploreView(APIView):
    permission_classes = [IsAuthenticated]

    def get(self, request):
        blocked_ids = blocked_user_ids(request.user)

        posts = (
            Post.objects
            .filter(
                author__is_active=True,
            )
            .exclude(
                author_id__in=blocked_ids,
            )
            .order_by("-created_at")
            .distinct()[:30]
        )

        hashtags = (
            Hashtag.objects
            .annotate(
                post_count=Count(
                    "posts",
                    distinct=True,
                )
            )
            .order_by("-post_count")[:10]
        )

        users = get_user_suggestions(
            request.user,
            limit=10,
        )

        return Response({
            "posts": SearchPostSerializer(
                posts,
                many=True,
            ).data,

            "hashtags": [
                {
                    "name": tag.name,
                    "post_count": tag.post_count,
                }
                for tag in hashtags
            ],

            "suggested_users": SearchUserSerializer(
                users,
                many=True,
            ).data,
        })
```

URL:

```python
path(
    "explore/",
    ExploreView.as_view(),
    name="explore",
),
```

API:

```text
GET /api/explore/
```

# 17.16 — Search Service

Create:

`frontend/src/services/searchService.js`

```javascript
import api from "./api";

export async function searchUsers(query) {
  const response = await api.get("/users/search/", {
    params: { q: query },
  });

  return response.data;
}

export async function searchPosts(query) {
  const response = await api.get("/posts/search/", {
    params: { q: query },
  });

  return response.data;
}

export async function getSuggestions() {
  const response = await api.get(
    "/users/suggestions/"
  );

  return response.data;
}

export async function getTrendingHashtags() {
  const response = await api.get(
    "/hashtags/trending/"
  );

  return response.data;
}

export async function getHashtag(tag) {
  const response = await api.get(
    `/hashtags/${encodeURIComponent(tag)}/`
  );

  return response.data;
}

export async function getExplore() {
  const response = await api.get(
    "/explore/"
  );

  return response.data;
}
```

# 17.17 — Debounced Search

You already have:

```text
frontend/src/hooks/useDebounce.js
```

Use it in Search.

`frontend/src/pages/Search.jsx`

```jsx
import { useEffect, useState } from "react";
import { useSearchParams } from "react-router-dom";

import useDebounce from "../hooks/useDebounce";
import {
  searchUsers,
  searchPosts,
} from "../services/searchService";

export default function Search() {
  const [params] = useSearchParams();

  const query = params.get("q") || "";

  const debouncedQuery = useDebounce(
    query,
    400
  );

  const [users, setUsers] = useState([]);
  const [posts, setPosts] = useState([]);
  const [loading, setLoading] = useState(false);

  useEffect(() => {
    if (debouncedQuery.length < 2) {
      setUsers([]);
      setPosts([]);
      return;
    }

    let cancelled = false;

    async function loadResults() {
      try {
        setLoading(true);

        const [
          userResponse,
          postResponse,
        ] = await Promise.all([
          searchUsers(debouncedQuery),
          searchPosts(debouncedQuery),
        ]);

        if (cancelled) return;

        setUsers(userResponse.results || []);
        setPosts(postResponse.results || []);
      } finally {
        if (!cancelled) {
          setLoading(false);
        }
      }
    }

    loadResults();

    return () => {
      cancelled = true;
    };
  }, [debouncedQuery]);

  return (
    <main className="search-page">
      <h1>Search</h1>

      {loading && <p>Searching...</p>}

      <section>
        <h2>People</h2>

        {users.map((user) => (
          <div key={user.id}>
            {user.username}
          </div>
        ))}
      </section>

      <section>
        <h2>Posts</h2>

        {posts.map((post) => (
          <article key={post.id}>
            <strong>
              {post.author.username}
            </strong>

            <p>{post.content}</p>
          </article>
        ))}
      </section>
    </main>
  );
}
```

# 17.20 — Explore Page

Create:

`frontend/src/pages/Explore.jsx`

```jsx
import { useEffect, useState } from "react";

import {
  getExplore,
} from "../services/searchService";

import TrendingCard from "../components/search/TrendingCard";
import SuggestedUserCard from "../components/search/SuggestedUserCard";

export default function Explore() {
  const [data, setData] = useState({
    posts: [],
    hashtags: [],
    suggested_users: [],
  });

  const [loading, setLoading] = useState(true);

  useEffect(() => {
    let mounted = true;

    async function loadExplore() {
      try {
        const response = await getExplore();

        if (mounted) {
          setData(response);
        }
      } finally {
        if (mounted) {
          setLoading(false);
        }
      }
    }

    loadExplore();

    return () => {
      mounted = false;
    };
  }, []);

  if (loading) {
    return <div>Loading explore...</div>;
  }

  return (
    <main className="explore-page">
      <h1>Explore</h1>

      <section>
        <h2>Trending</h2>

        <div>
          {data.hashtags.map((hashtag) => (
            <TrendingCard
              key={hashtag.name}
              hashtag={hashtag}
            />
          ))}
        </div>
      </section>

      <section>
        <h2>People You May Know</h2>

        <div>
          {data.suggested_users.map((user) => (
            <SuggestedUserCard
              key={user.id}
              user={user}
              onAdd={() => {}}
            />
          ))}
        </div>
      </section>

      <section>
        <h2>Explore Posts</h2>

        {data.posts.map((post) => (
          <article key={post.id}>
            <strong>
              @{post.author.username}
            </strong>

            <p>{post.content}</p>
          </article>
        ))}
      </section>
    </main>
  );
}
```

The `onAdd={() => {}}` should later be connected to your existing friend service.

---

# 17.21 — Hashtag Page

Create:

`frontend/src/pages/Hashtag.jsx`

```jsx
import { useEffect, useState } from "react";
import {
  useParams,
} from "react-router-dom";

import {
  getHashtag,
} from "../services/searchService";

export default function Hashtag() {
  const { tag } = useParams();

  const [data, setData] = useState({
    hashtag: tag,
    posts: [],
  });

  const [loading, setLoading] = useState(true);

  useEffect(() => {
    let mounted = true;

    async function load() {
      try {
        const response = await getHashtag(tag);

        if (mounted) {
          setData(response);
        }
      } finally {
        if (mounted) {
          setLoading(false);
        }
      }
    }

    load();

    return () => {
      mounted = false;
    };
  }, [tag]);

  if (loading) {
    return <div>Loading hashtag...</div>;
  }

  return (
    <main className="hashtag-page">
      <h1>
        #{data.hashtag}
      </h1>

      {data.posts.length === 0 ? (
        <p>No posts found.</p>
      ) : (
        data.posts.map((post) => (
          <article key={post.id}>
            <strong>
              @{post.author.username}
            </strong>

            <p>{post.content}</p>
          </article>
        ))
      )}
    </main>
  );
}
```

# 17.23 — Navbar Search

Your navbar should send users to:

```text
/search?q=keyword
```

Example:

```jsx
import { useState } from "react";
import { useNavigate } from "react-router-dom";

export default function SearchBar() {
  const [query, setQuery] = useState("");

  const navigate = useNavigate();

  function handleSubmit(event) {
    event.preventDefault();

    const value = query.trim();

    if (!value) return;

    navigate(
      `/search?q=${encodeURIComponent(value)}`
    );
  }

  return (
    <form onSubmit={handleSubmit}>
      <input
        type="search"
        value={query}
        placeholder="Search White Fiber"
        onChange={(event) =>
          setQuery(event.target.value)
        }
      />

      <button type="submit">
        Search
      </button>
    </form>
  );
}
```

---

# 17.24 — Hashtag Links Inside Posts

Make hashtags clickable.

Create:

`frontend/src/utils/renderHashtags.js`

```javascript
export function splitHashtags(text) {
  return text.split(
    /(#\w+)/g
  );
}
```

A component can then render:

```jsx
import { Link } from "react-router-dom";
import { splitHashtags } from "../utils/renderHashtags";

export default function PostContent({ content }) {
  return (
    <p>
      {splitHashtags(content).map(
        (part, index) => {
          if (!part.startsWith("#")) {
            return (
              <span key={index}>
                {part}
              </span>
            );
          }

          const tag = part.slice(1);

          return (
            <Link
              key={index}
              to={`/hashtag/${tag}`}
            >
              {part}
            </Link>
          );
        }
      )}
    </p>
  );
}
```

Then use:

```jsx
<PostContent content={post.content} />
```

instead of directly displaying:

```jsx
<p>{post.content}</p>
```

# 17.26 — Search UX

The final search flow should be:

```text
User types
     ↓
SearchBar
     ↓
/search?q=...
     ↓
400ms debounce
     ↓
┌───────────────────────┐
│ Search Users          │
│ Search Posts          │
└───────────────────────┘
     ↓
Search results
```

This prevents a request for every individual keystroke.

---

# 17.27 — Search Suggestions Dropdown

Later, your navbar can show quick results while typing:

```text
Search White Fiber
────────────────────────────

People
👤 john_doe
👤 john_smith

Posts
"Learning Django with React..."

# Hashtags
#django
#react

View all results →
```

The important part is that **search result visibility is still enforced by Django**.

React should never be treated as the security boundary.

---

# 17.28 — Privacy-Aware Discovery

Search must respect:

```text
Blocked users
       ↓
excluded

Suspended users
       ↓
excluded

Inactive users
       ↓
excluded

Private profiles
       ↓
permission checked

Existing friends
       ↓
excluded from suggestions

Pending requests
       ↓
excluded from suggestions
```

This should also be applied to:

```text
Explore
Search
Hashtags
Suggestions
Profiles
Feed
Comments
Messages
WebSocket
```

You already established blocking in Part 15, so **do not duplicate blocking logic in every view** if it can be centralized.

---

# 17.29 — Better Trending Algorithm

The first version counts recent posts.

For a more useful production system, define:

```text
score =
    recent_posts × 1
  + reactions × 2
  + comments × 3
```

with a time decay.

For example:

```text
score = engagement / age_factor
```

This should be implemented as a backend service rather than JavaScript.

Create later:

```text
backend/discovery/
└── trending.py
```

That gives you one place to improve the algorithm without rewriting the frontend.

---

# 17.30 — Pagination

For large White Fiber installations, do **not** return hundreds or thousands of posts.

Use your existing DRF pagination.

For example:

```text
GET /api/posts/search/?q=react&page=1
GET /api/posts/search/?q=react&page=2
GET /api/hashtags/react/?page=1
```

The response should follow your existing pagination format.

If your current search endpoint manually slices:

```python
posts[:20]
```

replace that with your standard DRF pagination before production.

---

# 17.31 — Backend Tests

Create:

```text
backend/discovery/tests.py
```

Example:

```python
from django.urls import reverse
from rest_framework.test import APITestCase

from django.contrib.auth import get_user_model

User = get_user_model()


class DiscoveryTests(APITestCase):

    def setUp(self):
        self.user = User.objects.create_user(
            username="alice",
            password="StrongPassword123",
        )

        self.other = User.objects.create_user(
            username="alice_other",
            password="StrongPassword123",
        )

        self.client.force_authenticate(
            user=self.user
        )

    def test_user_search(self):
        response = self.client.get(
            "/api/users/search/?q=alice"
        )

        self.assertEqual(
            response.status_code,
            200,
        )

        self.assertTrue(
            len(response.data["results"]) >= 1
        )

    def test_short_search(self):
        response = self.client.get(
            "/api/users/search/?q=a"
        )

        self.assertEqual(
            response.status_code,
            200,
        )

        self.assertEqual(
            response.data["results"],
            [],
        )

    def test_suggestions(self):
        response = self.client.get(
            "/api/users/suggestions/"
        )

        self.assertEqual(
            response.status_code,
            200,
        )

        self.assertIn(
            "results",
            response.data,
        )
```

Add tests for:

```text
✓ user search
✓ post search
✓ empty query
✓ short query
✓ hashtag lookup
✓ trending hashtags
✓ suggestions
✓ blocked users excluded
✓ suspended users excluded
✓ inactive users excluded
✓ private-profile permissions
✓ pagination
```

								PART 7
# Part 18 — Advanced Personalized Feed, Ranking & “For You”

Part 18 upgrades White Fiber from a basic chronological feed into a **personalized feed system**.

We will add:

* 🏠 Home feed ranking
* ⭐ “For You” feed
* 👥 Friend/activity weighting
* ❤️ Reaction/comment engagement weighting
* 🕒 Recency decay
* 🧠 Personalized scoring
* 🚫 Block/privacy filtering
* 📄 Cursor pagination
* ⚡ Efficient database queries
* 🔄 Feed refresh
* 🧪 Feed ranking tests

The frontend remains completely separate from Django:

```text
white-fiber/
├── frontend/   # React only
└── backend/    # Django only
```

---

# 18.1 — Feed Architecture

The new flow will be:

```text
User opens Home
        ↓
GET /api/feed/
        ↓
Django gets eligible posts
        ↓
Privacy filtering
        ↓
Block filtering
        ↓
Friend relationship
        ↓
Engagement
        ↓
Recency
        ↓
Feed score
        ↓
Rank posts
        ↓
Paginated response
        ↓
React renders PostCard
```

The important rule is:

> **Never calculate the authoritative feed ranking in React.**

React only displays what Django returns.

---

# 18.2 — Create Feed App

Create:

```text
backend/feed/
├── __init__.py
├── admin.py
├── apps.py
├── serializers.py
├── urls.py
├── views.py
├── services.py
├── ranking.py
├── permissions.py
└── tests.py
```

Create the app:

```bash
cd white-fiber/backend
python manage.py startapp feed
```

Add it to:

```python
INSTALLED_APPS = [
    # ...

    "feed",
]
```

---

# 18.3 — Feed Ranking Formula

We will start with a transparent scoring model.

For every eligible post:

```text
score =
    relationship_score
    + engagement_score
    + recency_score
    + content_score
```

Example:

```text
relationship_score
    friend post       = 30
    non-friend post   = 5

engagement_score
    reactions × 2
    comments × 3

recency_score
    decreases as post gets older

content_score
    small boost for posts with media
```

This is **not machine learning yet**.

It is much easier to debug and tune.

---

# 18.4 — Ranking Service

Create:

`backend/feed/ranking.py`

```python
from math import exp

from django.utils import timezone


def calculate_recency_score(created_at):
    age_hours = (
        timezone.now() - created_at
    ).total_seconds() / 3600

    return 30 * exp(-age_hours / 24)


def calculate_engagement_score(
    reaction_count,
    comment_count,
):
    return (
        reaction_count * 2
        + comment_count * 3
    )


def calculate_relationship_score(
    is_friend,
    is_following=False,
):
    score = 5

    if is_friend:
        score += 30

    if is_following:
        score += 15

    return score


def calculate_content_score(has_media):
    return 5 if has_media else 0


def calculate_post_score(
    post,
    is_friend,
    is_following=False,
):
    reaction_count = getattr(
        post,
        "reaction_count",
        0,
    )

    comment_count = getattr(
        post,
        "comment_count",
        0,
    )

    has_media = getattr(
        post,
        "has_media",
        False,
    )

    return (
        calculate_relationship_score(
            is_friend=is_friend,
            is_following=is_following,
        )
        + calculate_engagement_score(
            reaction_count=reaction_count,
            comment_count=comment_count,
        )
        + calculate_recency_score(
            post.created_at
        )
        + calculate_content_score(
            has_media=has_media
        )
    )
```

---

# 18.5 — Important: Feed Eligibility

Before ranking, Django must determine whether a post can be shown.

A post should be excluded if:

```text
❌ Author blocked user
❌ User blocked author
❌ Author suspended
❌ Author inactive
❌ Private post user isn't allowed to see
❌ Deleted post
```

So:

```text
Eligibility → Ranking
```

not:

```text
Ranking → Eligibility
```

This prevents private/blocked content from accidentally entering the feed.

---

# 18.6 — Block Filtering

Create:

`backend/feed/permissions.py`

```python
from moderation.models import UserBlock


def get_blocked_user_ids(user):
    blocked_by_me = UserBlock.objects.filter(
        blocker=user
    ).values_list(
        "blocked_id",
        flat=True,
    )

    blocked_me = UserBlock.objects.filter(
        blocked=user
    ).values_list(
        "blocker_id",
        flat=True,
    )

    return set(blocked_by_me).union(
        blocked_me
    )
```

---

# 18.7 — Friend IDs

Your existing friendship model should be used.

Add:

`backend/feed/services.py`

```python
from django.db.models import Q

from friends.models import Friendship


def get_friend_ids(user):
    friendships = Friendship.objects.filter(
        Q(user1=user) | Q(user2=user)
    ).values_list(
        "user1_id",
        "user2_id",
    )

    friend_ids = set()

    for user_one_id, user_two_id in friendships:
        if user_one_id != user.id:
            friend_ids.add(user_one_id)

        if user_two_id != user.id:
            friend_ids.add(user_two_id)

    return friend_ids
```

If your existing friendship model uses different field names, keep the logic but use those existing names.

---

# 18.8 — Efficient Post Query

Now create the main feed query.

`backend/feed/services.py`

```python
from django.db.models import (
    Count,
    Exists,
    OuterRef,
    Q,
)

from posts.models import Post

from .permissions import (
    get_blocked_user_ids,
)


def get_eligible_posts(user):
    blocked_ids = get_blocked_user_ids(user)

    posts = (
        Post.objects
        .select_related("author")
        .annotate(
            reaction_count=Count(
                "reactions",
                distinct=True,
            ),
            comment_count=Count(
                "comments",
                distinct=True,
            ),
        )
        .filter(
            author__is_active=True,
        )
        .exclude(
            author_id__in=blocked_ids,
        )
        .exclude(
            author_id=user.id,
        )
        .order_by("-created_at")
    )

    return posts
```

We deliberately use:

```python
select_related("author")
```

to avoid repeated author queries.

---

# 18.9 — Include Your Own Posts

A user's own posts should normally be allowed in the home feed.

Change:

```python
.exclude(author_id=user.id)
```

to nothing.

So:

```python
posts = (
    Post.objects
    .select_related("author")
    .annotate(
        reaction_count=Count(
            "reactions",
            distinct=True,
        ),
        comment_count=Count(
            "comments",
            distinct=True,
        ),
    )
    .filter(
        author__is_active=True,
    )
    .exclude(
        author_id__in=blocked_ids,
    )
)
```

Your own posts are now eligible.

---

# 18.10 — Privacy Filtering

Your existing `Post` visibility system should be applied here.

For example, if your model has:

```python
visibility
```

with:

```text
public
friends
private
```

then the queryset should use:

```python
from django.db.models import Q


visibility_filter = (
    Q(visibility="public")
    | Q(
        visibility="friends",
        author_id__in=friend_ids,
    )
    | Q(
        author_id=user.id,
    )
)
```

Then:

```python
posts = posts.filter(
    visibility_filter
)
```

The exact field/value names should match your existing Part 2/3 post model.

---

# 18.11 — Feed Scoring

Now rank the posts.

Add:

```python
from .ranking import calculate_post_score
from .services import get_friend_ids
```

Then:

```python
def rank_posts(posts, user):
    friend_ids = get_friend_ids(user)

    ranked_posts = []

    for post in posts:
        is_friend = (
            post.author_id in friend_ids
        )

        score = calculate_post_score(
            post=post,
            is_friend=is_friend,
        )

        ranked_posts.append(
            (score, post)
        )

    ranked_posts.sort(
        key=lambda item: (
            item[0],
            item[1].created_at,
        ),
        reverse=True,
    )

    return [
        post
        for score, post in ranked_posts
    ]
```

---

# 18.12 — Feed Service

Create the complete service:

`backend/feed/services.py`

```python
from django.db.models import (
    Count,
    Q,
)

from posts.models import Post
from friends.models import Friendship

from moderation.models import UserBlock

from .permissions import (
    get_blocked_user_ids,
)

from .ranking import (
    calculate_post_score,
)


def get_friend_ids(user):
    friendships = (
        Friendship.objects
        .filter(
            Q(user1=user)
            | Q(user2=user)
        )
        .values_list(
            "user1_id",
            "user2_id",
        )
    )

    friend_ids = set()

    for user_one_id, user_two_id in friendships:
        if user_one_id != user.id:
            friend_ids.add(user_one_id)

        if user_two_id != user.id:
            friend_ids.add(user_two_id)

    return friend_ids


def get_eligible_posts(user):
    blocked_ids = get_blocked_user_ids(user)
    friend_ids = get_friend_ids(user)

    visibility_filter = (
        Q(visibility="public")
        | Q(
            visibility="friends",
            author_id__in=friend_ids,
        )
        | Q(author_id=user.id)
    )

    return (
        Post.objects
        .select_related("author")
        .annotate(
            reaction_count=Count(
                "reactions",
                distinct=True,
            ),
            comment_count=Count(
                "comments",
                distinct=True,
            ),
        )
        .filter(
            visibility_filter,
            author__is_active=True,
        )
        .exclude(
            author_id__in=blocked_ids,
        )
        .order_by("-created_at")
    )


def rank_posts(posts, user):
    friend_ids = get_friend_ids(user)

    ranked = []

    for post in posts:
        score = calculate_post_score(
            post=post,
            is_friend=(
                post.author_id
                in friend_ids
            ),
        )

        ranked.append(
            (score, post)
        )

    ranked.sort(
        key=lambda item: (
            item[0],
            item[1].created_at,
        ),
        reverse=True,
    )

    return [
        post
        for score, post in ranked
    ]
```

# 18.13 — Feed Serializer

```python
from rest_framework import serializers

from django.contrib.auth import get_user_model

from posts.models import Post

User = get_user_model()


class FeedUserSerializer(
    serializers.ModelSerializer
):
    class Meta:
        model = User
        fields = [
            "id",
            "username",
            "first_name",
            "last_name",
            "avatar",
            "is_online",
        ]


class FeedPostSerializer(
    serializers.ModelSerializer
):
    author = FeedUserSerializer(
        read_only=True
    )

    reaction_count = serializers.IntegerField(
        read_only=True
    )

    comment_count = serializers.IntegerField(
        read_only=True
    )

    class Meta:
        model = Post
        fields = [
            "id",
            "author",
            "content",
            "created_at",
            "updated_at",
            "reaction_count",
            "comment_count",
        ]
```

# 18.14 — Feed API

Create:

`backend/feed/views.py`

```python
from rest_framework.views import APIView
from rest_framework.permissions import IsAuthenticated
from rest_framework.response import Response

from .services import (
    get_eligible_posts,
    rank_posts,
)

from .serializers import (
    FeedPostSerializer,
)


class FeedView(APIView):
    permission_classes = [
        IsAuthenticated,
    ]

    def get(self, request):
        posts = get_eligible_posts(
            request.user
        )

        ranked_posts = rank_posts(
            posts[:200],
            request.user,
        )

        return Response({
            "results": FeedPostSerializer(
                ranked_posts[:20],
                many=True,
            ).data,
        })
```

---

# 18.16 — Why Limit to 200 Before Ranking?

This:

```python
posts[:200]
```

is intentional for the first production version.

You don't want:

```text
100,000 posts
       ↓
Python
       ↓
calculate 100,000 scores
```

Instead:

```text
database
   ↓
recent eligible candidates
   ↓
200 candidates
   ↓
ranking
   ↓
20 posts
```

Later, Part 18 can be upgraded to a database-side scoring system.

---

```text
HOME

FOR YOU
```

```python
class ForYouFeedView(APIView):

    ]

    def get(self, request):

            request.user
        )

        ranked_posts = rank_posts(
            posts[:300],

            "results": FeedPostSerializer(
                ranked_posts[:20],
```

# 18.19 — Improve Ranking With Interactions

The next level is tracking user interactions.

Create:

```text
backend/feed/models.py
```

```python
from django.conf import settings
from django.db import models


class FeedInteraction(models.Model):
    class InteractionType(
        models.TextChoices
    ):
        VIEW = "view", "View"
        LIKE = "like", "Like"
        COMMENT = "comment", "Comment"
        SHARE = "share", "Share"
        SAVE = "save", "Save"

    user = models.ForeignKey(
        settings.AUTH_USER_MODEL,
        on_delete=models.CASCADE,
        related_name="feed_interactions",
    )

    post = models.ForeignKey(
        "posts.Post",
        on_delete=models.CASCADE,
        related_name="feed_interactions",
    )

    interaction_type = models.CharField(
        max_length=20,
        choices=InteractionType.choices,
    )

    created_at = models.DateTimeField(
        auto_now_add=True
    )

    class Meta:
        indexes = [
            models.Index(
                fields=[
                    "user",
                    "created_at",
                ]
            ),
            models.Index(
                fields=[
                    "post",
                    "interaction_type",
                ]
            ),
        ]
```

Run:

```bash
python manage.py makemigrations
python manage.py migrate
```

# 18.20 — Interaction Scoring

Now we can identify what a user interacts with.

Example:

```text
User frequently interacts with:
        ↓
posts by Alice
        ↓
Alice gets relationship boost

User frequently likes:
        ↓
#django
        ↓
#django content gets interest boost
```

Create:

`backend/feed/interaction_service.py`

```python
from django.db.models import Count

from .models import FeedInteraction


def get_user_interaction_weights(user):
    interactions = (
        FeedInteraction.objects
        .filter(user=user)
        .values(
            "interaction_type"
        )
        .annotate(
            count=Count("id")
        )
    )

    return {
        item["interaction_type"]:
            item["count"]
        for item in interactions
    }
```

---

# 18.21 — Don't Over-Personalize

A good social feed should not become:

```text
User likes one topic
        ↓
100% of feed becomes that topic
```

Instead maintain diversity:

```text
60% relevant
20% friends
10% discovery
10% fresh/random
```

The exact proportions should be configurable.

Create:

```python
FEED_DIVERSITY = {
    "relevant": 0.60,
    "friends": 0.20,
    "discovery": 0.10,
    "fresh": 0.10,
}
```

This gives you a future configuration point.

```javascript
    "/feed/",

  params = {}
) {
```

# 18.23 — Feed Hook

Create:

`frontend/src/hooks/useFeed.js`

```javascript
import {
  useCallback,
  useEffect,
  useState,
} from "react";

import {
  getFeed,
} from "../services/feedService";

export default function useFeed() {
  const [posts, setPosts] = useState([]);
  const [loading, setLoading] =
    useState(true);

  const [refreshing, setRefreshing] =
    useState(false);

  const loadFeed = useCallback(
    async () => {
      try {
        setLoading(true);

        const data = await getFeed();

        setPosts(
          data.results || []
        );
      } finally {
        setLoading(false);
      }
    },
    []
  );

  const refresh = useCallback(
    async () => {
      try {
        setRefreshing(true);

        const data = await getFeed();

        setPosts(
          data.results || []
        );
      } finally {
        setRefreshing(false);
      }
    },
    []
  );

  useEffect(() => {
    loadFeed();
  }, [loadFeed]);

  return {
    posts,
    loading,
    refreshing,
    refresh,
  };
}
```

# 18.24 — Home Feed

Update:

`frontend/src/pages/Home.jsx`

```jsx
import useFeed from "../hooks/useFeed";
import PostCard from "../components/posts/PostCard";

export default function Home() {
  const {
    posts,
    loading,
    refreshing,
    refresh,
  } = useFeed();

  if (loading) {
    return (
      <main>
        <p>Loading feed...</p>
      </main>
    );
  }

  return (
    <main className="home-page">
      <header>
        <h1>Home</h1>

        <button
          type="button"
          onClick={refresh}
          disabled={refreshing}
        >
          {refreshing
            ? "Refreshing..."
            : "Refresh"}
        </button>
      </header>

      {posts.length === 0 ? (
        <p>
          No posts available yet.
        </p>
      ) : (
        posts.map((post) => (
          <PostCard
            key={post.id}
            post={post}
          />
        ))
      )}
    </main>
  );
}
```

# 18.26 — For You Page

Create:

`frontend/src/pages/ForYou.jsx`

```jsx
import { useEffect, useState } from "react";

import {
  getForYouFeed,
} from "../services/feedService";

import PostCard from "../components/posts/PostCard";

export default function ForYou() {
  const [posts, setPosts] =
    useState([]);

  const [loading, setLoading] =
    useState(true);

  useEffect(() => {
    let mounted = true;

    async function load() {
      try {
        const data =
          await getForYouFeed();

        if (mounted) {
          setPosts(
            data.results || []
          );
        }
      } finally {
        if (mounted) {
          setLoading(false);
        }
      }
    }

    load();

    return () => {
      mounted = false;
    };
  }, []);

  if (loading) {
    return (
      <main>
        Loading...
      </main>
    );
  }

  return (
    <main>
      <h1>For You</h1>

      {posts.map((post) => (
        <PostCard
          key={post.id}
          post={post}
        />
      ))}
    </main>
  );
}
```

# 18.28 — Feed Interaction Tracking

When a post becomes visible, record a view.

Use your existing Intersection Observer infrastructure.

Create:

`frontend/src/hooks/usePostView.js`

```javascript
import { useEffect, useRef } from "react";
import api from "../services/api";

export default function usePostView(
  postId
) {
  const elementRef = useRef(null);
  const sentRef = useRef(false);

  useEffect(() => {
    const element =
      elementRef.current;

    if (!element || sentRef.current) {
      return;
    }

    const observer =
      new IntersectionObserver(
        async ([entry]) => {
          if (
            entry.isIntersecting &&
            !sentRef.current
          ) {
            sentRef.current = true;

            try {
              await api.post(
                `/feed/${postId}/view/`
              );
            } catch (error) {
              console.error(
                "Failed to record post view",
                error
              );
            }

            observer.disconnect();
          }
        },
        {
          threshold: 0.5,
        }
      );

    observer.observe(element);

    return () => {
      observer.disconnect();
    };
  }, [postId]);

  return elementRef;
}
```
