---

# 36. Story Privacy

Current Part 22 supports:

```text
Public
Friends
```

Rules:

### Public

```text
Anyone who can access the account's content
        ↓
can view Story
```

### Friends

```text
Friendship exists
        ↓
Story visible
```

### Blocked

```text
User A blocks User B

A's Stories → B cannot see
B's Stories → A cannot see
```

This is enforced server-side.

---

# 37. Suspended Users

Your existing suspension system must also apply.

Do not rely on:

```jsx
if (user.is_suspended)
```

in React.

The Django Story API must enforce it.

Add to the relevant authenticated permission flow if your project does not already reject suspended users globally.

The server should prevent:

```text
Suspended user
    ↓
create Story
view Story
view other users' Stories
```

according to your existing account-suspension policy.

---

# 38. Story Notifications

Do **not** notify every friend every time somebody posts a Story.

That would create excessive notifications.

For Part 22, the recommended behavior is:

```text
Story created
     ↓
No mass push notification
     ↓
Story appears in Story Tray
```

Later, an optional:

```text
"Notify me about this user's Stories"
```

preference can be added.

---

# 39. Story Viewers

We already track:

```text
StoryView
```

This enables a future feature:

```text
Your Story
     ↓
Viewed by 14 people
     ↓
John
Sarah
Alex
...
```

For privacy reasons, only the Story owner should be able to see its viewer list.

Add this endpoint now.

## MODIFY `backend/stories/views.py`

```python
class StoryViewerListView(APIView):
    permission_classes = [IsAuthenticated]

    def get(self, request, story_id):
        story = get_object_or_404(
            Story,
            id=story_id,
            owner=request.user,
        )

        viewers = (
            StoryView.objects
            .filter(story=story)
            .select_related("viewer")
            .order_by("-viewed_at")
        )

        results = [
            {
                "id": view.viewer.id,
                "username": view.viewer.username,
                "first_name": view.viewer.first_name,
                "last_name": view.viewer.last_name,
                "avatar": (
                    view.viewer.avatar.url
                    if view.viewer.avatar
                    else None
                ),
                "viewed_at": view.viewed_at,
            }
            for view in viewers
        ]

        return Response({
            "results": results,
        })
```

Then import it in `urls.py` and add:

```python
path(
    "<int:story_id>/viewers/",
    StoryViewerListView.as_view(),
    name="story-viewers",
),
```

API:

```text
GET /api/stories/<id>/viewers/
```

Only the Story owner can access it.

---

# 40. Story Deletion

When a user deletes:

```text
Story
```

the `StoryView` records disappear automatically because of:

```python
on_delete=models.CASCADE
```

The underlying `MediaAsset` should **not automatically be deleted** unless your media lifecycle system confirms it is unused elsewhere.

This is important because the same media asset could potentially be referenced elsewhere.

---

# 41. Story Media Cleanup

Eventually you should have a media cleanup worker:

```text
MediaAsset
    ↓
Is it referenced by:
    Post?
    Message?
    Story?
    Other?
       ↓
No references
       ↓
Delete safely
```

Do not delete MediaAsset immediately from Story deletion.

# 44. Story Tests

## NEW FILE — `backend/stories/tests.py`

```python
from datetime import timedelta

from django.contrib.auth import get_user_model
from django.utils import timezone

from rest_framework.test import APITestCase

from media.models import MediaAsset
from .models import Story, StoryView


User = get_user_model()


class StoryTests(APITestCase):

    def setUp(self):
        self.user = User.objects.create_user(
            username="john",
            email="john@example.com",
            password="StrongPassword123!",
        )

        self.friend = User.objects.create_user(
            username="sarah",
            email="sarah@example.com",
            password="StrongPassword123!",
        )

        self.media = MediaAsset.objects.create(
            owner=self.user,
            media_type=MediaAsset.MediaType.IMAGE,
            file="media/test.jpg",
            processing_status=(
                MediaAsset.ProcessingStatus.READY
            ),
        )

    def authenticate(self, user):
        self.client.force_authenticate(
            user=user
        )

    def test_create_story(self):
        self.authenticate(self.user)

        response = self.client.post(
            "/api/stories/create/",
            {
                "media_id": self.media.id,
                "visibility": "friends",
            },
        )

        self.assertEqual(
            response.status_code,
            201,
        )

        self.assertEqual(
            Story.objects.count(),
            1,
        )

    def test_story_expires_after_24_hours(self):
        story = Story.objects.create(
            owner=self.user,
            media=self.media,
            expires_at=(
                timezone.now()
                - timedelta(minutes=1)
            ),
        )

        self.authenticate(self.friend)

        response = self.client.get(
            "/api/stories/"
        )

        self.assertEqual(
            response.status_code,
            200,
        )

        story_ids = [
            item["id"]
            for item in response.data["results"]
        ]

        self.assertNotIn(
            story.id,
            story_ids,
        )

    def test_story_view_is_created(self):
        story = Story.objects.create(
            owner=self.user,
            media=self.media,
        )

        self.authenticate(self.friend)

        response = self.client.post(
            f"/api/stories/{story.id}/view/"
        )

        self.assertEqual(
            response.status_code,
            200,
        )

        self.assertTrue(
            StoryView.objects.filter(
                story=story,
                viewer=self.friend,
            ).exists()
        )

    def test_owner_can_delete_story(self):
        story = Story.objects.create(
            owner=self.user,
            media=self.media,
        )

        self.authenticate(self.user)

        response = self.client.delete(
            f"/api/stories/{story.id}/delete/"
        )

        self.assertEqual(
            response.status_code,
            204,
        )

    def test_other_user_cannot_delete_story(self):
        story = Story.objects.create(
            owner=self.user,
            media=self.media,
        )

        self.authenticate(self.friend)

        response = self.client.delete(
            f"/api/stories/{story.id}/delete/"
        )

        self.assertEqual(
            response.status_code,
            404,
        )
```

Adapt the `MediaAsset` creation fields to your exact Part 19 model if your implementation requires additional mandatory fields.

# 2. Password Change

## NEW FILE

```text
backend/users/security_views.py
```

```python
from django.contrib.auth import authenticate
from django.contrib.auth import get_user_model

from rest_framework import status
from rest_framework.permissions import IsAuthenticated
from rest_framework.response import Response
from rest_framework.views import APIView


User = get_user_model()


class ChangePasswordView(APIView):
    permission_classes = [IsAuthenticated]

    def post(self, request):
        current_password = request.data.get(
            "current_password"
        )

        new_password = request.data.get(
            "new_password"
        )

        if not current_password or not new_password:
            return Response(
                {
                    "detail": (
                        "Current and new passwords "
                        "are required."
                    )
                },
                status=status.HTTP_400_BAD_REQUEST,
            )

        if not request.user.check_password(
            current_password
        ):
            return Response(
                {
                    "detail": "Current password is incorrect."
                },
                status=status.HTTP_400_BAD_REQUEST,
            )

        if len(new_password) < 8:
            return Response(
                {
                    "detail": (
                        "Password must be at least "
                        "8 characters."
                    )
                },
                status=status.HTTP_400_BAD_REQUEST,
            )

        request.user.set_password(new_password)
        request.user.save(
            update_fields=["password"]
        )

        return Response({
            "message": "Password changed successfully."
        })
```

---

# 3. Password Validation

## MODIFY EXISTING FILE

`backend/config/settings.py`

```python
AUTH_PASSWORD_VALIDATORS = [
    {
        "NAME": (
            "django.contrib.auth."
            "password_validation."
            "UserAttributeSimilarityValidator"
        ),
    },
    {
        "NAME": (
            "django.contrib.auth."
            "password_validation."
            "MinimumLengthValidator"
        ),
    },
    {
        "NAME": (
            "django.contrib.auth."
            "password_validation."
            "CommonPasswordValidator"
        ),
    },
    {
        "NAME": (
            "django.contrib.auth."
            "password_validation."
            "NumericPasswordValidator"
        ),
    },
]
```

Then improve the password change view to use Django's validator.

## MODIFY `security_views.py`

Add:

```python
from django.contrib.auth.password_validation import (
    validate_password,
)
```

Before `set_password()`:

```python
try:
    validate_password(
        new_password,
        request.user,
    )
except Exception as exc:
    return Response(
        {
            "detail": exc.messages
        },
        status=status.HTTP_400_BAD_REQUEST,
    )
```

# 6. Change Password UI

```jsx
<form onSubmit={handlePasswordChange}>
  <input
    type="password"
    placeholder="Current password"
    value={currentPassword}
    onChange={(event) =>
      setCurrentPassword(event.target.value)
    }
  />

  <input
    type="password"
    placeholder="New password"
    value={newPassword}
    onChange={(event) =>
      setNewPassword(event.target.value)
    }
  />

  <input
    type="password"
    placeholder="Confirm new password"
    value={confirmPassword}
    onChange={(event) =>
      setConfirmPassword(event.target.value)
    }
  />

  <button type="submit">
    Change password
  </button>
</form>
```

Before submitting:

```javascript
if (newPassword !== confirmPassword) {
  setError("Passwords do not match.");
  return;
}
```

# 9. Never Store Passwords Yourself

White Fiber should **never** store:

```text
plain password
password in localStorage
password in logs
password in analytics
```

Django handles password hashing.

Frontend only sends the password over:

```text
HTTPS
```

---

# 10. Sensitive Data Protection

Never return:

```text
password
password hash
refresh token
access token
Firebase service-account credentials
database password
SECRET_KEY
```

from an API.

For example, user serializer should expose:

```python
fields = [
    "id",
    "username",
    "first_name",
    "last_name",
    "avatar",
    "is_online",
    "last_seen",
]
```

not sensitive authentication fields.

# 11. Privacy Settings

```text
public
friends
private
```

If your existing model uses different values, **keep the existing choices** rather than creating duplicate fields.

# 12. Privacy Model

Conceptually:

```text
PUBLIC
   ↓
Anyone eligible can see profile

FRIENDS
   ↓
Only friends

PRIVATE
   ↓
Only owner / explicitly allowed interactions
```

Server-side enforcement is required.

React should not decide privacy.

---

# 13. Profile Permission Service

## NEW FILE

```text
backend/users/privacy.py
```

```python
from friends.models import Friendship
from moderation.models import UserBlock


def are_friends(user_a, user_b):
    return Friendship.objects.filter(
        user1=user_a,
        user2=user_b,
    ).exists() or Friendship.objects.filter(
        user1=user_b,
        user2=user_a,
    ).exists()


def is_blocked(user_a, user_b):
    return (
        UserBlock.objects.filter(
            blocker=user_a,
            blocked=user_b,
        ).exists()
        or UserBlock.objects.filter(
            blocker=user_b,
            blocked=user_a,
        ).exists()
    )


def can_view_profile(viewer, target):
    if viewer.id == target.id:
        return True

    if is_blocked(viewer, target):
        return False

    visibility = target.profile_visibility

    if visibility == "public":
        return True

    if visibility == "friends":
        return are_friends(
            viewer,
            target,
        )

    return False
```

### Important

If your `Friendship` model doesn't use:

```text
user1
user2
```

then use the actual existing field names.

---

# 14. Online Status Privacy

Users should be able to control whether others can see:

```text
Online
Last seen
```

Add to User if it doesn't already exist:

```python
show_online_status = models.BooleanField(
    default=True
)

show_last_seen = models.BooleanField(
    default=True
)
```

You already have:

```python
is_online
last_seen
```

so these are privacy controls around those existing fields.

---

# 15. Serializer Protection

## MODIFY EXISTING USER SERIALIZER

Instead of always returning:

```python
"is_online",
"last_seen",
```

calculate them according to privacy.

Example:

```python
def get_is_online(self, obj):
    request = self.context.get("request")

    if not request:
        return False

    if not obj.show_online_status:
        return False

    return obj.is_online
```

And:

```python
def get_last_seen(self, obj):
    if not obj.show_last_seen:
        return None

    return obj.last_seen
```

# 16. Message Privacy

Part 21 already prevents unauthorized users from entering conversations.

Part 23 reinforces:

```text
Blocked user
    ↓
cannot start conversation
    ↓
cannot send message
    ↓
cannot WebSocket connect
```

Your existing block check must remain on:

```text
POST /api/messages/conversations/
POST /api/messages/conversations/<id>/messages/
WebSocket /ws/chat/<id>/
```

---

# 17. WebSocket Security

Your WebSocket must check all of these:

```text
JWT valid?
     ↓
User active?
     ↓
User suspended?
     ↓
Conversation exists?
     ↓
User is member?
     ↓
Block relationship?
     ↓
ALLOW
```

Do **not** only check JWT.

# 18. Suspended Account Protection

Your existing User model has:

```python
is_suspended
suspended_until
```

Create a centralized helper.

## NEW FILE

```text
backend/users/account_status.py
```

```python
from django.utils import timezone


def is_user_suspended(user):
    if not user.is_suspended:
        return False

    if (
        user.suspended_until
        and user.suspended_until <= timezone.now()
    ):
        return False

    return True
```

---

# 19. Automatically Clear Expired Suspension

If suspension has expired:

```python
if (
    user.is_suspended
    and user.suspended_until
    and user.suspended_until <= timezone.now()
):
    user.is_suspended = False
    user.suspended_until = None
    user.save(
        update_fields=[
            "is_suspended",
            "suspended_until",
        ]
    )
```

Better long-term: perform this through a service/task rather than duplicating the logic everywhere.

---

# 20. Global Suspended User Permission

## NEW FILE

```text
backend/config/permissions.py
```

```python
from rest_framework.permissions import BasePermission

from users.account_status import is_user_suspended


class IsActiveAndNotSuspended(
    BasePermission
):
    message = (
        "Your account is currently unavailable."
    )

    def has_permission(
        self,
        request,
        view,
    ):
        user = request.user

        if not user or not user.is_authenticated:
            return False

        if not user.is_active:
            return False

        if is_user_suspended(user):
            return False

        return True
```

Use this on sensitive authenticated APIs.

Your existing settings already have:

```python
DEFAULT_THROTTLE_RATES = {
    "anon": "30/minute",
    "user": "120/minute",
```

Part 23 adds stricter limits for sensitive operations.

## MODIFY `settings.py`

```python
"DEFAULT_THROTTLE_RATES": {
    "anon": "30/minute",
    "user": "120/minute",
    "login": "10/minute",
    "register": "5/hour",
    "password": "5/hour",
    "message": "60/minute",
    "upload": "30/minute",
    "report": "10/hour",
    "admin": "300/minute",
},
```

# 22. Custom Throttles

## NEW FILE

```text
backend/config/throttles.py
```

```python
from rest_framework.throttling import (
    ScopedRateThrottle,
)


class LoginRateThrottle(
    ScopedRateThrottle
):
    scope = "login"


class RegisterRateThrottle(
    ScopedRateThrottle
):
    scope = "register"


class PasswordRateThrottle(
    ScopedRateThrottle
):
    scope = "password"


class MessageRateThrottle(
    ScopedRateThrottle
):
    scope = "message"


class UploadRateThrottle(
    ScopedRateThrottle
):
    scope = "upload"


class ReportRateThrottle(
    ScopedRateThrottle
):
    scope = "report"


class AdminRateThrottle(
    ScopedRateThrottle
):
    scope = "admin"
```

---

# 23. Login Protection

Apply:

```python
throttle_classes = [
    LoginRateThrottle,
]
```

to your login view.

This prevents unlimited password attempts.

If you already use a login serializer/view, **modify that existing view rather than creating a second login endpoint**.

---

# 24. Registration Protection

Apply:

```python
throttle_classes = [
    RegisterRateThrottle,
]
```

to registration.

This helps prevent automated account creation.

---

# 25. Password Protection

Apply:

```python
throttle_classes = [
    PasswordRateThrottle,
]
```

to:

```text
POST /api/users/me/change-password/
```

---

# 26. Message Spam Protection

Apply:

```python
throttle_classes = [
    MessageRateThrottle,
]
```

to message creation.

Remember:

**WebSocket typing events should not be persisted as messages.**

The existing ~1200ms typing debounce from Part 21 remains important.

---

# 27. Upload Protection

Apply:

```python
throttle_classes = [
    UploadRateThrottle,
]
```

to:

```text
POST /api/media/upload/
```

This protects your media endpoint from abuse.

---

# 28. Report Abuse Protection

Apply:

```python
throttle_classes = [
    ReportRateThrottle,
]
```

to:

```text
POST /api/moderation/reports/
```

Otherwise someone could generate thousands of fake reports.

---

# 29. Request Validation

Never trust frontend validation.

For every API:

```text
React validation
       ↓
Django validation
       ↓
Database constraints
```

Example:

```python
username = str(
    request.data.get("username", "")
).strip()
```

Then validate:

```text
length
format
allowed characters
uniqueness
permissions
ownership
```

---

# 30. Object-Level Authorization

This is extremely important.

Never do:

```python
post = Post.objects.get(
    id=post_id
)

post.delete()
```

for an authenticated request.

Instead:

```python
post = get_object_or_404(
    Post,
    id=post_id,
    author=request.user,
)
```

or explicitly check ownership/moderator permission.

Same principle applies to:

```text
Posts
Comments
Messages
Stories
Media
Reports
Profiles
```

---

# 31. Media Ownership

Part 19's protection remains:

```python
MediaAsset.objects.filter(
    id__in=media_ids,
    owner=request.user,
)
```

Never accept:

```text
media_id
```

and assume the user owns it.

---

# 34. CORS

Keep CORS restrictive.

Production should look like:

```python
CORS_ALLOWED_ORIGINS = [
    "https://whitefiber.com",
    "https://www.whitefiber.com",
]
```

Do **not** use:

```python
CORS_ALLOW_ALL_ORIGINS = True
```

in production.

---

# 35. CSRF

Keep:

```python
CSRF_TRUSTED_ORIGINS = [
    "https://whitefiber.com",
    "https://www.whitefiber.com",
    "https://api.whitefiber.com",
]
```

Do not use:

```text
*
```

for production trusted origins.

---

# 36. File Upload Security

Part 19 validates:

```text
JPEG
PNG
WebP
MP4
WebM
MOV
```

Part 23 adds another principle:

**Never trust the filename or MIME type alone.**

Example:

```text
malware.exe
renamed → photo.jpg
```

must not automatically be accepted.

Your media processing layer should inspect actual file content/signatures.

For production media processing:

```text
Upload
 ↓
Temporary storage
 ↓
Validate file signature
 ↓
Validate size
 ↓
Decode/process
 ↓
READY
```

---

# 37. Dangerous File Names

Never directly use a user-controlled filename as a storage path.

Use Django's:

```python
upload_to
```

plus generated storage names.

Do not construct:

```python
f"uploads/{request.data['filename']}"
```

---

# 38. Report System Improvement

Your existing Report model supports:

```text
spam
harassment
abuse
hate
sexual
violence
impersonation
scam
other
```

Add duplicate protection.

## MODIFY `Report`

A user shouldn't repeatedly submit identical reports for the same object.

For example, use application-level validation:

```python
existing = Report.objects.filter(
    reporter=request.user,
    reported_user=reported_user,
    status__in=[
        Report.Status.PENDING,
        Report.Status.REVIEWING,
    ],
).exists()
```

If already reported:

```python
return Response(
    {
        "detail": (
            "You already have an active "
            "report for this user."
        )
    },
    status=status.HTTP_400_BAD_REQUEST,
)
```

Apply the equivalent logic to Post and Comment reports.

---

# 39. Account Deletion Security

Existing:

```text
DELETE /api/users/me/delete/
```

should require deliberate confirmation.

Frontend:

```text
Delete Account
       ↓
Warning
       ↓
Type DELETE
       ↓
Confirm
       ↓
Django DELETE
```

Do not allow accidental one-click deletion.

---

# 40. Frontend Delete Confirmation

## MODIFY EXISTING

`frontend/src/pages/Settings.jsx`

Use:

```jsx
const [deleteConfirmation, setDeleteConfirmation] =
  useState("");
```

Enable deletion only when:

```javascript
deleteConfirmation === "DELETE"
```

Example:

```jsx
<input
  value={deleteConfirmation}
  onChange={(event) =>
    setDeleteConfirmation(
      event.target.value
    )
  }
  placeholder="Type DELETE"
/>

<button
  disabled={
    deleteConfirmation !== "DELETE"
  }
>
  Delete account
</button>
```

# 41. Security Activity

For production, add an audit/security log.

## NEW FILE

```text
backend/users/security_models.py
```

```python
from django.conf import settings
from django.db import models


class SecurityEvent(models.Model):
    class EventType(models.TextChoices):
        LOGIN = "login", "Login"
        LOGIN_FAILED = "login_failed", "Login Failed"
        PASSWORD_CHANGED = (
            "password_changed",
            "Password Changed",
        )
        ACCOUNT_DELETED = (
            "account_deleted",
            "Account Deleted",
        )
        TOKEN_REFRESH = (
            "token_refresh",
            "Token Refresh",
        )

    user = models.ForeignKey(
        settings.AUTH_USER_MODEL,
        on_delete=models.SET_NULL,
        null=True,
        blank=True,
        related_name="security_events",
    )

    event_type = models.CharField(
        max_length=40,
        choices=EventType.choices,
    )

    ip_address = models.GenericIPAddressField(
        null=True,
        blank=True,
    )

    user_agent = models.TextField(
        blank=True,
    )

    created_at = models.DateTimeField(
        auto_now_add=True,
    )

    class Meta:
        ordering = ["-created_at"]
        indexes = [
            models.Index(
                fields=["user", "-created_at"]
            ),
            models.Index(
                fields=[
                    "event_type",
                    "-created_at",
                ]
            ),
        ]
```

---

# 42. Don't Store Excessive Security Data

Security logs should **not** store:

```text
password
JWT token
refresh token
Firebase private key
credit card information
```

IP/user-agent retention should also follow your privacy/retention policy.

---

# 43. Login Security Logging

After successful login:

```python
SecurityEvent.objects.create(
    user=user,
    event_type=(
        SecurityEvent.EventType.LOGIN
    ),
    ip_address=(
        request.META.get("REMOTE_ADDR")
    ),
    user_agent=(
        request.META.get(
            "HTTP_USER_AGENT",
            ""
        )
    ),
)
```

For failed authentication, log carefully without storing the submitted password.

from the browser.

# 49. API Error Messages

Don't expose internal errors.

Bad:

```json
{
  "error": "Post.DoesNotExist at /api/posts/123/"
}
```

Better:

```json
{
  "detail": "Post not found."
}
```

Do not return:

```text
database SQL
Python traceback
filesystem paths
secret configuration
```

to users.

---

## MODIFY EXISTING FILE

```env
DJANGO_DEBUG=False
```

Production must never run with:

```text
DEBUG=True
```

---

# 51. Django Secret Key

Production:

```env
DJANGO_SECRET_KEY=long-random-secret
```

Never:

```python
SECRET_KEY = "django-insecure-..."
```

in production.

---

# 52. Database Security

Production database should:

```text
not be publicly exposed
require strong password
use private network
use encrypted connection where appropriate
have backups
```

Your existing Docker setup should keep PostgreSQL internal.

For example:

```text
Internet
   ↓
Nginx
   ↓
Django
   ↓
PostgreSQL
```

not:

```text
Internet
   ↓
PostgreSQL :5432
```

---

# 53. Redis Security

Same principle:

```text
Internet ❌
     ↓
Redis
```

Redis should be accessible only to trusted application/worker services.

---

# 54. Celery Security

Your existing Celery workers now handle:

```text
media processing
push notifications
story cleanup
```

Make sure Celery/Redis aren't publicly exposed.

---

# 55. Frontend Token Storage

Your existing authentication implementation should be reviewed carefully.

Avoid putting sensitive long-lived credentials into:

```text
localStorage
```

where possible.

For browser production, a common approach is:

```text
short-lived access token
+
secure refresh mechanism
```

For your Capacitor Android app, token storage should use secure native storage rather than plain browser storage when you introduce that integration.

# 56. Android Security

For production Android:

```text
HTTPS
WSS
```

only.

Don't use:

```text
http://api.whitefiber.com
ws://api.whitefiber.com
```

in production.

Also don't enable:

```xml
android:usesCleartextTraffic="true"
```

unless there is a specific development requirement.

# 57. Security Headers Through Nginx

## MODIFY EXISTING NGINX

Inside HTTPS server configuration, add appropriate headers such as:

```nginx
add_header X-Content-Type-Options "nosniff" always;
add_header X-Frame-Options "DENY" always;
add_header Referrer-Policy "strict-origin-when-cross-origin" always;
```

Don't blindly add a restrictive Content-Security-Policy until your actual frontend assets/API/WebSocket requirements have been tested.

# Part 24 — Performance + Scaling + Production Optimization

Part 24 ka goal hai White Fiber ko **zyada users, posts, messages, media aur concurrent WebSocket connections** handle karne ke liye optimize karna.

Sabse important rule:

> **Existing code ko rewrite nahi karna.** Jahan existing implementation already hai, usko optimize/extend karna hai.

# 2. First Rule — Don't Optimize Everything at Once

Production optimization ka basic rule:

```text
Measure
   ↓
Find bottleneck
   ↓
Optimize
   ↓
Measure again
```

Randomly indexes/cache add karna useful nahi hota.

---

# 3. Database Indexes

Tumhare project mein sabse important database tables:

```text
User
Post
Comment
Reaction
Friendship
Conversation
Message
Notification
Story
StoryView
MediaAsset
Report
FeedInteraction
```

In tables par frequently queried fields indexed hone chahiye.

---

# 4. Post Indexes

## MODIFY EXISTING

`backend/posts/models.py`

Agar already indexes nahi hain, Post ke `Meta` mein:

```python
class Meta:
    ordering = ["-created_at"]

    indexes = [
        models.Index(
            fields=["author", "-created_at"]
        ),
        models.Index(
            fields=["visibility", "-created_at"]
        ),
        models.Index(
            fields=["-created_at"]
        ),
    ]
```

Agar existing `Meta` mein indexes hain, **duplicate indexes mat banana**.

---

# 5. Comment Indexes

Comments ke liye:

```python
class Meta:
    ordering = ["created_at"]

    indexes = [
        models.Index(
            fields=["post", "created_at"]
        ),
        models.Index(
            fields=["author", "-created_at"]
        ),
    ]
```

```python
indexes = [
```

Chat pagination ke liye ye especially important hai.

---

# 11. `select_related()` Use Karo

Bad:

```python
posts = Post.objects.all()

for post in posts:
    print(post.author.username)
```

Isse unnecessary queries ho sakti hain.

Better:

```python
posts = (
    Post.objects
    .select_related("author")
    .all()
)
```

Tumhare Feed mein already:

```python
.select_related("author")
```

hai.

**Keep it.**

---

# 12. `prefetch_related()` Use Karo

Many-to-many/reverse relations ke liye:

```python
posts = (
    Post.objects
    .select_related("author")
    .prefetch_related(
        "media_assets",
    )
)
```

Agar serializer reactions/comments access karta hai, appropriate prefetch add karo.

Lekin har relation ko blindly prefetch mat karo.

---

# 13. Avoid N+1 Queries

Ye pattern avoid karo:

```python
for post in posts:
    post.comments.count()
    post.reactions.count()
```

Instead:

```python
from django.db.models import Count

posts = posts.annotate(
    comment_count=Count(
        "comments",
        distinct=True,
    ),
    reaction_count=Count(
        "reactions",
        distinct=True,
    ),
)
```

Part 18 Feed mein ye already use ho raha hai.

```text
Posts
 ↓

sorted result
```

Small/medium scale par acceptable hai.

Lekin millions of posts par:

```text
      ↓
```

isliye candidate selection strict rakho.

# 16. Feed Cache

Personalized feeds can be expensive.

Use Redis for short-lived feed caching.

## NEW FILE

```text
backend/feed/cache.py
```

```python
from django.core.cache import cache


FEED_CACHE_TIMEOUT = 30


def feed_cache_key(user_id):
    return f"feed:user:{user_id}"


def get_cached_feed(user_id):
    return cache.get(
        feed_cache_key(user_id)
    )


def set_cached_feed(user_id, data):
    cache.set(
        feed_cache_key(user_id),
        data,
        FEED_CACHE_TIMEOUT,
    )


def invalidate_feed(user_id):
    cache.delete(
        feed_cache_key(user_id)
    )
```

---

# 17. Don't Cache Sensitive Personalized Data Too Long

Feed cache:

```text
30 seconds
```

is reasonable as a starting point.

Don't cache personalized content for hours.

Also ensure cached data is scoped to the authenticated user.

---

# 18. Feed Invalidation

When a user creates a post:

```text
New Post
 ↓
invalidate relevant feed caches
```

At minimum invalidate the author's own feed.

For friend feeds, fan-out invalidation can become expensive at scale.

Start simple.

# 19. API Response Caching

Good candidates:

```text

explore
public configuration
some discovery data
```

Bad candidates:

```text
private messages
private notifications
private profiles
personalized feed
```

unless cache keys and permissions are handled correctly.

```python
from django.core.cache import cache

def get_trending_cache():
```

# 24. Message Pagination

Part 21 recommended:

```text

     ↓

     ↓

```

This should be cursor-based where possible.

Example API:

```text

```

Do not load the entire conversation.

---

# 25. Notification Pagination

Never do:

```python
Notification.objects.filter(
    recipient=request.user
)
```

and serialize thousands of notifications.

Use pagination:

```python
notifications = (
    Notification.objects
    .filter(
        recipient=request.user
    )
    .order_by("-created_at")
)
```

Then let DRF pagination limit the response.

---

# 26. Story Pagination

Stories are usually small because they expire after 24 hours.

Still, avoid returning unrelated expired records.

Current:

```python

```

is correct.

# 27. Database Connection Pooling

Production Django with many requests can benefit from connection pooling.

If you're using PostgreSQL through Docker, consider a dedicated connection pooler such as PgBouncer when traffic actually requires it.

Architecture:

```text
Django
  │
  ▼
PgBouncer
  │
  ▼
PostgreSQL
```

Don't add unnecessary infrastructure before measuring connection pressure.

# 28. Gunicorn/Daphne Workers

Your deployment uses ASGI/WebSockets.

Typical architecture:

```text
Nginx
  │
  ├── HTTP → ASGI/Django
  │
  └── WS → Daphne/ASGI
```

For production, worker count should be based on available CPU/RAM and workload.

Don't blindly choose a huge number.

Messages can be distributed between instances.

using your existing configuration.

# 30. WebSocket Connection Cleanup

Frontend must close sockets.

Existing pattern:

```javascript
useEffect(() => {
  const socket = new WebSocket(url);

  return () => {
    socket.close();
  };
}, [url]);
```

Never leave WebSockets open after navigating away.

---

# 31. WebSocket Reconnect

Add controlled reconnect behavior.

## NEW FILE

```text
frontend/src/hooks/useWebSocketReconnect.js
```

```javascript
import { useEffect, useRef } from "react";

export function useWebSocketReconnect(
  url,
  onMessage
) {
  const socketRef = useRef(null);
  const retryRef = useRef(null);

  useEffect(() => {
    let stopped = false;
    let retryDelay = 1000;

    function connect() {
      if (stopped) {
        return;
      }

      const socket = new WebSocket(url);

      socketRef.current = socket;

      socket.onopen = () => {
        retryDelay = 1000;
      };

      socket.onmessage = (event) => {
        onMessage?.(
          JSON.parse(event.data)
        );
      };

      socket.onclose = () => {
        if (stopped) {
          return;
        }

        retryRef.current =
          window.setTimeout(() => {
            retryDelay = Math.min(
              retryDelay * 2,
              30000
            );

            connect();
          }, retryDelay);
      };
    }

    connect();

    return () => {
      stopped = true;

      if (retryRef.current) {
        window.clearTimeout(
          retryRef.current
        );
      }

      socketRef.current?.close();
    };
  }, [url, onMessage]);

  return socketRef;
}
```

For your existing chat hook, integrate this carefully rather than running a second WebSocket alongside the existing one.

---

# 32. Don't Reconnect Forever on Auth Failure

If WebSocket closes because:

```text
JWT expired
401/4401
```

don't reconnect indefinitely.

Instead:

```text
Token invalid
 ↓
Refresh authentication
 ↓
Reconnect
```

or logout if refresh fails.

As traffic grows, separate queues.

# 34. Celery Task Routing

## MODIFY EXISTING

Celery configuration:

```python
CELERY_TASK_ROUTES = {
    "media.tasks.*": {
        "queue": "media",
    },

    "notifications.tasks.*": {
        "queue": "notifications",
    },

    "stories.tasks.*": {
        "queue": "maintenance",
    },
}
```

Then run workers separately:

```bash
celery -A config worker -Q media
```

```bash
celery -A config worker -Q notifications
```

```bash
celery -A config worker -Q maintenance
```

For development, one worker can process all queues.

# 36. Object Storage

Production media should not live permanently inside:


Examples include S3-compatible storage or another managed object-storage provider.

The exact provider can be selected later without changing the application architecture.

Static/media delivery:

```text

 ↓
```

API:

```text

 ↓
Django
```

Don't route large videos through Django for every playback request if object storage/CDN can serve them directly.

# 40. Lazy Loading Components

React pages that aren't needed immediately can be lazy-loaded.

## MODIFY EXISTING

`frontend/src/routes/AppRoutes.jsx`

Example:

```jsx
import { lazy, Suspense } from "react";

const Messages = lazy(
  () => import("../pages/Messages")
);

const Notifications = lazy(
  () => import("../pages/Notifications")
);

const Settings = lazy(
  () => import("../pages/Settings")
);
```

Then:

```jsx
<Suspense fallback={<div>Loading...</div>}>
  <Routes>
    {/* existing routes */}
  </Routes>
</Suspense>
```

Don't lazy-load every tiny component.

For example, don't do:

# 47. Notification Polling

Part 20 had notification polling.

Don't poll aggressively.

Bad:

```text
every 1 second
```

Better:

```text

```

for fallback polling.

Real-time events should be used where available.

# 48. Push Notifications

For mobile:

```text
FCM Push
```

should be the primary background notification mechanism.

Don't keep an aggressive polling loop running in the background Android app.

# 49. Database Query Monitoring

During development, identify slow queries.

Django Debug Toolbar can be used in development only.

Do not expose Django Debug Toolbar in production.

# 50. Health Endpoint

Your Part 11 already has:

```text

```

Keep it.

A health endpoint should be lightweight.

Example:

```json

  "status": "ok"
}
```

---

# 51. Readiness vs Liveness

For larger deployments, use:

```text
/health/
/ready/
```

Conceptually:

```text
/health/
    ↓
Process alive?

/ready/
    ↓
Database available?
Redis available?
Application ready?
```

Don't make `/health/` perform expensive checks.

---

# 52. Database Health Check

A readiness check can verify DB connectivity.

But keep it separate from the basic liveness endpoint so a temporary DB outage doesn't make orchestration think the process itself has crashed.

---

# 53. Logging

Production logs should include:

```text
timestamp
level
request path
status code
request ID
duration
```

Example:

```text
2026-09-24 12:10:30 INFO
GET /api/feed/
200
duration=124ms
```

Do not log:

```text
password
JWT
refresh token
Firebase credentials
message private content
```

unless there is a specifically justified, privacy-reviewed diagnostic need.

---

# 54. Request ID

## NEW FILE

```text
backend/config/middleware.py
```

```python
import uuid


class RequestIDMiddleware:
    def __init__(self, get_response):
        self.get_response = get_response

    def __call__(self, request):
        request_id = request.headers.get(
            "X-Request-ID"
        ) or str(uuid.uuid4())

        request.request_id = request_id

        response = self.get_response(
            request
        )

        response["X-Request-ID"] = request_id

        return response
```

---

# 55. Enable Middleware

## MODIFY EXISTING

`backend/config/settings.py`

Add near the beginning of `MIDDLEWARE`:

```python
"config.middleware.RequestIDMiddleware",
```

For example:

```python
MIDDLEWARE = [
    "config.middleware.RequestIDMiddleware",

    # existing middleware...
]
```

# 56. Error Tracking

Production should have an error-tracking system.

Typical architecture:

```text
React
 ↓
Error tracking

Django
 ↓
Error tracking

Celery
 ↓
Error tracking
```

You can use a service such as Sentry later, but keep credentials in environment variables.

Do not hard-code DSNs/secrets into source code.

---

# 57. Frontend Error Boundary

## NEW FILE

```text
frontend/src/components/common/ErrorBoundary.jsx
```

```jsx
import React from "react";

export default class ErrorBoundary
  extends React.Component {
  constructor(props) {
    super(props);

    this.state = {
      hasError: false,
    };
  }

  static getDerivedStateFromError() {
    return {
      hasError: true,
    };
  }

  componentDidCatch(error, info) {
    console.error(
      "White Fiber UI error:",
      error,
      info
    );
  }

  render() {
    if (this.state.hasError) {
      return (
        <div className="error-boundary">
          <h2>
            Something went wrong.
          </h2>

          <button
            type="button"
            onClick={() =>
              window.location.reload()
            }
          >
            Reload
          </button>
        </div>
      );
    }

    return this.props.children;
  }
}
```

---

# 58. Add Error Boundary

## MODIFY EXISTING

`frontend/src/main.jsx`

Wrap the existing application:

```jsx
<ErrorBoundary>
  <App />
</ErrorBoundary>
```

Import:

```jsx
import ErrorBoundary from "./components/common/ErrorBoundary";
```

Keep your existing providers/router structure.

should not render 10,000 DOM nodes.

Infinite scrolling should not mean:

all remain mounted forever.

For very large feeds, consider virtualization.

can use:

when profiling shows unnecessary rerenders.

Don't wrap every component in `memo` without measuring.

For heavily repeated components, use:

# 65. Nginx Compression

Your Nginx should compress text assets.

Example:

```nginx
gzip on;
gzip_types
    text/plain
    text/css
    application/json
    application/javascript
    application/xml
    image/svg+xml;
```

Don't gzip already-compressed video/image formats unnecessarily.

---

# 66. Browser Caching

Static assets generated by Vite usually have hashed filenames.

Nginx can safely cache those assets for longer periods.

Example:

```nginx
location /assets/ {
    expires 1y;
    add_header Cache-Control "public, immutable";
}
```

Only use this for versioned/hash-named assets.

---

# 67. API Cache Headers

Don't globally cache authenticated API responses.

For example:

```text
/api/messages/
/api/notifications/
/api/feed/
```

should not receive a generic public cache policy.

---

# 68. Media Cache Headers

Public immutable media can use long cache durations.

Private media should use appropriate authorization/signed URL mechanisms.

This is especially important for:

```text
private Stories
private messages
private attachments
```

---

# 69. Database Backups

Your Part 11 backup system remains mandatory.

Production:

```text
PostgreSQL
   ↓
Automated backup
   ↓
Off-site storage
```

Test restoration periodically.

A backup that has never been restored/tested should not be assumed reliable.

---

# 70. Redis Persistence

Redis is primarily used for:

```text
cache
channels
Celery
```

Don't make your application depend on Redis cache data being permanent.

Database remains the source of truth for persistent application data.

---

# 71. Media Backup

Object storage should have its own durability/backup strategy.

Don't rely only on:

```text
Docker volume
```

for user-uploaded photos/videos.

---

# 72. Scaling Architecture

When White Fiber becomes larger:

```text
                    ┌───────────────┐
                    │     CDN       │
                    └───────┬───────┘
                            │
                         Nginx/LB
                            │
              ┌─────────────┴─────────────┐
              │                           │
        Django/ASGI 1              Django/ASGI 2
              │                           │
              └─────────────┬─────────────┘
                            │
                          Redis
                       /          \
                Celery             Channels
                   │
                   │
              PostgreSQL
                   │
             Object Storage
```

This is the direction, not something you need to deploy immediately.

---

# 73. Database Read Replicas

At much larger scale:

```text
Writes
 ↓
Primary PostgreSQL

Reads
 ↓
Read replicas
```

But don't introduce read replicas until actual database workload justifies them.

---

# 74. Search Scaling

Current search:

```text

icontains
```

is acceptable for early scale.

At larger scale:

```text
PostgreSQL
     ↓
Search engine
```

could be introduced.

Don't add Elasticsearch/OpenSearch now unless needed.

---

# 75. Feed Scaling

At very large scale, Python ranking of 200 candidates per request can still become expensive.

Possible future architecture:

```text
User interactions
       ↓
Ranking features
       ↓
Precomputed candidate feed
       ↓
Redis
       ↓
API
```

But your current Part 18 candidate-limit implementation is a reasonable foundation.

---

# 76. Stories Scaling

Story tray only needs active Stories:

```python
Story.objects.filter(
    expires_at__gt=timezone.now()
)
```

With the `expires_at` index, this remains efficient.

Expired Stories are removed by Celery.

# 77. Notification Scaling

Current:

```text

 ↓
```

is correct.

At larger scale:

```text

 ↓

 ↓

 ↓

```

can be introduced.

Part 20 should remove invalid Firebase tokens when Firebase reports a permanent invalid-registration error.

# 81. Suggested Targets

These are **engineering targets**, not guarantees:

```text

< 200ms target

Feed:
< 500ms target

Search:
< 500ms target

Message send:
< 300ms target

WebSocket message:
low-latency / near real-time

Image upload:
depends heavily on file size/network

Video processing:
asynchronous
```

Measure actual production performance rather than treating these as promises.

Before public launch, perform load testing in a **non-production environment**.

```text

1,000 concurrent requests
WebSocket connections
media uploads

message traffic
```

The actual test numbers should reflect your expected launch traffic and infrastructure capacity.


or equivalent staging environment.

Never run aggressive load tests against your live production database/API without a controlled plan.

```text
              │                     │
        React + Vite          Django/ASGI
              │                     │
      ┌───────┼───────┐       ┌─────┼─────┐
      │       │       │       │     │     │
    Cache   Lazy    Images   DB   Redis Celery
    UI     Load     CDN      │     │      │
                             │     │      │
                         PostgreSQL │   Workers
                                    │
                              WebSockets
                                    │
                              Notifications
```

---

								PART 14

# Part 25 — Final QA + Google Play Release + Production Launch

This is the **final phase** of White Fiber.

We will not rewrite the existing application. Part 25 is a **release-hardening phase**: verify everything from Parts 1–24, fix only release blockers, produce the signed Android App Bundle, complete Play Console requirements, and launch safely.

One important update from the earlier Android configuration: **as of August 31, 2026, new apps and app updates submitted to Google Play must target Android 16 / API 36 or higher**. So the earlier `targetSdk 35` example must now be changed to `36` for White Fiber. ([Android Developers][1])

# 25.21 — Moderation QA

# 25.23 — Account Deletion

This must be tested end-to-end.

Your existing:

```text
DELETE /api/users/me/delete/
```

must remove/anonymize the appropriate records according to your retention design.

Check:

```text
User
Devices
Posts
Comments
Reactions
Friends
Messages
Notifications
Blocks
Reports
Media ownership
Stories
Security events
```

Do not leave an active login session that continues working after deletion.

Google Play currently requires an in-app account deletion path **and a web resource where users can request account deletion and associated data deletion** when the app allows account creation. ([Google Support][3])

So White Fiber should have something like:

```text
https://whitefiber.com/delete-account
```

That page must actually work.

or the corresponding Gradle configuration used by your Capacitor version.

to:

or, depending on your existing Gradle syntax:

```gradle
targetSdkVersion 36
```

For White Fiber's September 2026 Play submission, target API 36 is the relevant requirement. Google states that from August 31, 2026, new apps and updates must target Android 16/API 36 or higher. ([Android Developers][1])

Also make sure API 36 is installed in Android Studio/SDK Manager.

# 25.36 — Play Console Testing Requirement

If your Play Console account is a **personal developer account created after November 13, 2023**, Google currently requires a closed test with at least **12 testers opted in continuously for at least 14 days** before applying for production access. ([Google Support][4])

The process is:

```text
Internal testing
      ↓
Closed testing
      ↓
12+ testers
      ↓
14 continuous days
      ↓
Apply for production access
      ↓
Production
```

The testers must remain opted in continuously; dropping below the requirement or breaking the continuous period can affect eligibility. ([Google Support][4])

---

# 25.37 — Play Console Device Verification

For new personal developer accounts, Google also has a device-verification requirement involving access to a real Android mobile device through the Play Console mobile app. ([Google Support][5])

Complete this if it appears in your Play Console dashboard.

# 25.52 — Final API Security Test

Try unauthorized requests against:

```text
Users
Posts
Comments
Friends
Messages
Notifications
Media
Stories
Admin
Moderation
Feed
Discovery
```

Expected behavior:

```text
401 → unauthenticated

404 → resource not exposed where appropriate
400 → invalid request
429 → throttled
```

Never expose:

```text

SQL query
filesystem path
secret key
JWT
Firebase credential
password hash
private message
```

```text
database dumps
node_modules
__pycache__
```

Then:

```bash
git diff
```

Review every release change manually.

---

# 25.54 — Final Build Commands

## Backend

```bash
cd white-fiber/backend

python manage.py makemigrations --check
python manage.py migrate
python manage.py check
python manage.py check --deploy
python manage.py test
python manage.py collectstatic --noinput
```

## Frontend

```bash
cd white-fiber/frontend

npm install
npm run test:run
npm run build
```

## Android

```bash
npx cap sync android
npx cap open android
```

Then:

```bash
cd android
./gradlew clean
./gradlew bundleRelease
```

# 25.55 — Final Release Artifact

Your final artifact should be:

```text
frontend/android/app/build/outputs/bundle/release/app-release.aab
```

Before uploading, verify the application ID:

```text
com.whitefiber.app
```

and:

```text
targetSdk = 36
```

---

# 25.56 — Google Play Production Release

Once all required testing/setup is complete:

```text
Play Console
    ↓
White Fiber
    ↓
Test and release
    ↓
Production
    ↓
Create new release
    ↓
Upload AAB
    ↓
Review
    ↓
Submit for review
```

Google's current release workflow supports internal, closed, open and production tracks, with production releases becoming available to users in the selected countries/regions after rollout. ([Google Support][6])

Do not describe White Fiber as "on the Play Store" until Google has actually approved and published the production release.

---

# 25.57 — Recommended First Production Rollout

For the first public launch:

```text
AAB
 ↓
Play Console review
 ↓
Production
 ↓
Controlled rollout
 ↓
Monitor
 ↓
Increase availability
```

Watch:

```text
Crash rate
ANR rate
Login failures
API 5xx
WebSocket failures
Push failures
Media failures
Celery failures
Database load
Redis memory
User reports
```

---

# 25.58 — Rollback Plan

If a serious production problem occurs:

```text
Detect issue
    ↓
Stop rollout / reduce exposure
    ↓
Identify bad version
    ↓
Restore previous stable backend/frontend
    ↓
Fix issue
    ↓
Increment Android versionCode
    ↓
Build new AAB
    ↓
Internal test
    ↓
Closed test if needed
    ↓
Production update
```

Never reuse an old Android `versionCode` for a new AAB.

---

					FINAL PRODUCTION

Bilkul. Neeche **White Fiber ka single end-to-end Final Launch Guide** hai — local project se lekar **Google Play Store par publicly visible app** tak.

Main is guide mein existing Parts 1–25 ko repeat nahi kar raha; sirf **exact launch sequence** de raha hoon. Aur current September 2026 Play requirements ko verify kiya hai: new apps/updates ko **Android 16 / API 36+** target karna hai, aur qualifying new personal developer accounts ko production se pehle **12 testers + 14 continuous days closed testing** complete karni hoti hai. ([Android Developers][1])

---

# WHITE FIBER — FINAL LAUNCH GUIDE

## Phase 0 — Tumhare paas kya hona chahiye

Project:

```text
white-fiber/
├── frontend/
└── backend/
```

Production domains:

```text
whitefiber.com
www.whitefiber.com
api.whitefiber.com
```

Production API:

```text
https://api.whitefiber.com/api
```

Production WebSocket:

```text
wss://api.whitefiber.com/ws
```

Android package:

```text
com.whitefiber.app
```

---

# PHASE 1 — Local machine prepare karo

## 1. Backend directory

```bash
cd white-fiber/backend
```

Virtual environment activate karo.

### Windows

```bash
venv\Scripts\activate
```

### Linux/macOS

```bash
source venv/bin/activate
```

---

## 2. Backend dependencies

```bash
pip install -r requirements.txt
```

Agar requirements file complete nahi hai, ensure your project has the packages already used by White Fiber, including Django, DRF, Channels, SimpleJWT, PostgreSQL driver, Redis/Celery dependencies, Pillow and Firebase Admin.

---

# PHASE 2 — Backend verify karo

Run:

```bash
python manage.py makemigrations --check
```

Agar output clean hai, next:

```bash
python manage.py migrate
```

Then:

```bash
python manage.py check
```

Then:

```bash
python manage.py check --deploy
```

Then:

```bash
python manage.py test
```

### Expected

```text
System check identified no issues
...
OK
```

### Agar tests fail hon

**Yahin ruk jao.**

Pehle failing test fix karo.

Play Store par jaane se pehle backend tests pass hone chahiye.

---

# PHASE 3 — Production database

Production PostgreSQL create/configure karo.

Example:

```env
DATABASE_URL=postgres://whitefiber:YOUR_PASSWORD@db:5432/whitefiber
```

Production mein:

```env
DJANGO_DEBUG=False
```

Aur:

```env
DJANGO_SECRET_KEY=LONG_RANDOM_SECRET
```

Use karo.

Secret ko GitHub/Git mein mat daalna.

---

# PHASE 4 — Redis

Production Redis configure karo:

```env
REDIS_URL=redis://redis:6379/0
```

Redis publicly accessible **nahi** hona chahiye.

Redis White Fiber mein multiple roles use karega:

```text
Redis
├── Django cache
├── Celery
└── Channels/WebSockets
```

---

# PHASE 5 — Firebase

Firebase project mein Android app ka package:

```text
com.whitefiber.app
```

register karo.

Download:

```text
google-services.json
```

Place:

```text
frontend/android/app/google-services.json
```

Backend Firebase service account ko:

```text
backend/secrets/firebase-service-account.json
```

mein rakho.

Production environment:

```env
FIREBASE_CREDENTIALS_PATH=/app/secrets/firebase-service-account.json
```

### Important

Ye files GitHub par **upload nahi honi chahiye**.

---

# PHASE 6 — Production backend environment

Production `.env` ko approximately:

```env
DJANGO_SECRET_KEY=YOUR_LONG_RANDOM_SECRET
DJANGO_DEBUG=False

DJANGO_ALLOWED_HOSTS=api.whitefiber.com,whitefiber.com,www.whitefiber.com

CSRF_TRUSTED_ORIGINS=https://whitefiber.com,https://www.whitefiber.com,https://api.whitefiber.com

CORS_ALLOWED_ORIGINS=https://whitefiber.com,https://www.whitefiber.com

DATABASE_URL=postgres://whitefiber:YOUR_PASSWORD@db:5432/whitefiber

REDIS_URL=redis://redis:6379/0

TIME_ZONE=Asia/Karachi

FIREBASE_CREDENTIALS_PATH=/app/secrets/firebase-service-account.json
```

---

# PHASE 7 — Django production security

`backend/config/settings.py` verify karo:

```python
DEBUG = False

SECURE_PROXY_SSL_HEADER = (
    "HTTP_X_FORWARDED_PROTO",
    "https",
)

SESSION_COOKIE_SECURE = True
CSRF_COOKIE_SECURE = True

SECURE_SSL_REDIRECT = True

SECURE_HSTS_SECONDS = 31536000
SECURE_HSTS_INCLUDE_SUBDOMAINS = True
SECURE_HSTS_PRELOAD = True

SECURE_CONTENT_TYPE_NOSNIFF = True
X_FRAME_OPTIONS = "DENY"
SECURE_REFERRER_POLICY = "strict-origin-when-cross-origin"
```

Aur:

```python
CORS_ALLOW_ALL_ORIGINS = False
```

---

# PHASE 8 — Static files

Run:

```bash
python manage.py collectstatic --noinput
```

Verify static files successfully generate ho rahe hain.

---

# PHASE 9 — Domain + DNS

Apne domain provider mein DNS configure karo.

Conceptually:

```text
whitefiber.com
       ↓
Frontend server

www.whitefiber.com
       ↓
Frontend server

api.whitefiber.com
       ↓
Backend server
```

Agar Docker/server setup use kar rahe ho, DNS ko apne server ke public IP/load balancer par point karo.

---

# PHASE 10 — HTTPS

Let's Encrypt/Certbot ya equivalent certificate setup karo.

Final URLs:

```text
https://whitefiber.com
https://www.whitefiber.com
https://api.whitefiber.com
```

HTTP ko HTTPS par redirect karo.

---

# PHASE 11 — Nginx

Nginx ko approximately:

```text
/
    → React frontend

/api/
    → Django

/ws/
    → Django Channels

/static/
    → static files

/media/
    → media/object storage
```

route karna hai.

WebSocket ke liye:

```nginx
proxy_http_version 1.1;
proxy_set_header Upgrade $http_upgrade;
proxy_set_header Connection "upgrade";
```

hona chahiye.

---

# PHASE 12 — Backend production start

Tumhare deployment architecture ke mutabiq Django ASGI/Daphne/Gunicorn setup start karo.

Channels use ho raha hai, isliye WebSocket traffic ASGI server tak correctly jana chahiye.

Phir Celery:

```bash
celery -A config worker -Q media
```

```bash
celery -A config worker -Q notifications
```

```bash
celery -A config worker -Q maintenance
```

Development mein single worker acceptable ho sakta hai; production mein queues separate karna better hai.

hona chahiye.

# PHASE 29 — Release signing key

Agar abhi tak key nahi banayi:

```bash
keytool -genkeypair \
  -v \
  -keystore ~/white-fiber-keystore/white-fiber-release.jks \
  -keyalg RSA \
  -keysize 2048 \
  -validity 10000 \
  -alias white-fiber
```

Is key ko **extremely safely backup** karo.

Lose hone se future Android updates complicated ho sakte hain.

Never commit:

```text
*.jks
*.keystore
```

---

# PHASE 30 — Release Gradle secrets

Agar tumhara signing configuration `gradle.properties` use karta hai:

```properties
WHITE_FIBER_KEYSTORE_FILE=/absolute/path/to/white-fiber-release.jks
WHITE_FIBER_KEYSTORE_PASSWORD=YOUR_SECRET
WHITE_FIBER_KEY_ALIAS=white-fiber
WHITE_FIBER_KEY_PASSWORD=YOUR_SECRET
```

Ye file Git mein nahi honi chahiye.

---

# PHASE 40 — Closed testing

Agar tumhara Play developer account **13 November 2023 ke baad created personal account** hai, Google currently requires:

```text
minimum 12 testers
+
14 continuous days
```

before production access. ([Google Support][6])

Flow:

```text
Internal testing
       ↓
Closed testing
       ↓
12 testers
       ↓
14 continuous days
       ↓
Apply for production access
```

Agar tumhara account is category mein nahi aata, Play Console tumhare applicable requirements ke mutabiq flow show karega.

```text
Read exact policy issue
        ↓
Fix app/listing
        ↓
Increment versionCode if required
        ↓
Build new AAB
        ↓
Upload
        ↓
Submit again
```

---

# PHASE 45 — PUBLIC VISIBILITY

Jab production release Google approve karke publish karega:

```text
White Fiber
       ↓
Google Play
       ↓
Public production
       ↓
Users can find/install app
```

Google ke production track ka purpose hi app ko selected countries/regions mein Google Play users ke liye available karna hai. ([Google Support][5])

**Isi point par White Fiber officially Play Store par live/visible hogi.**

---

# PHASE 46 — Launch ke first 24 hours

Launch ke baad continuously monitor:

```text
API 5xx
Database
Redis
Celery
WebSocket
Firebase
Push failures
Media processing
Crash/ANR
Login failures
Reports
User feedback
```

---

# PHASE 47 — First production backup

Launch ke baad database backup:

```bash
pg_dump "$DATABASE_URL" > white-fiber-production-backup.sql
```

Secure location par store karo.

Backup restore test bhi karo.

---

# PHASE 48 — Future update process

Har future White Fiber update:

```text
Code change
    ↓
Backend tests
    ↓
Frontend tests
    ↓
npm build
    ↓
Android testing
    ↓
versionCode +1
    ↓
new AAB
    ↓
Internal testing
    ↓
Closed testing if applicable/needed
    ↓
Production
```

Example:

```text
1.0.0 / code 1
       ↓
1.0.1 / code 2
       ↓
1.1.0 / code 3
       ↓
1.2.0 / code 4
```

# 🎯 Tumhara actual final path

Isko bas ek line mein yaad rakho:

```text
WHITE FIBER CODE
      ↓
BACKEND TEST
      ↓
PRODUCTION SERVER
      ↓
DOMAIN + HTTPS/WSS
      ↓
FIREBASE
      ↓
REACT PRODUCTION BUILD
      ↓
CAPACITOR SYNC
      ↓
ANDROID API 36
      ↓
SIGNED AAB
      ↓
PLAY CONSOLE
      ↓
INTERNAL TEST
      ↓
CLOSED TEST
      ↓
12 TESTERS / 14 DAYS*
      ↓
PRODUCTION ACCESS*
      ↓
GOOGLE REVIEW
      ↓
APPROVAL
      ↓
🚀 WHITE FIBER LIVE ON GOOGLE PLAY
```

`*` applies to qualifying personal developer accounts created after November 13, 2023. ([Google Support][6])

**Bas yahi complete journey hai — project folder se lekar actual public Play Store app tak.**
